Reserve Bank of A ustralia  |  Statement on Monetar y Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026 67

### Page 71

Australian GDP growth is expected
to remain below our estimate of
potential growth throughout the
forecast period.
GDP growth has slowed since the end of 2025 and is
expected to remain subdued over the next couple of
years (Graph 3.3). The expected slowing in growth over
2026 reflects the effects of several headwinds weighing
on private activity, including the reduction in household
disposable incomes from higher inflation, weaker
conditions in the established housing market, and the

tightening in monetary policy, which more than offset
very strong data centre-related investment. Growth is
expected to remain below potential over the forecast
period, reflecting an assumption that financial
conditions remain restrictive (see Key judgement #2).
Consistent with the latest state and federal budgets,
public demand is expected to continue supporting
growth over the forecast period.
The risks to activity appear to be broadly balanced.

Stronger-than-expected global growth and further
strength in AI and data centre-related activity pose
upside risks to domestic growth (see Key risk #2).
However, historically low consumer and business
sentiment could be pointing to the risk that the
combined effect on domestic activity of the Middle East
conflict, the decline in housing prices and the tightening
in monetary policy could be more than assumed (see
Key risk #3).
The level of GDP at end-2028 is expected to be

broadly unchanged from the August forecasts.
Growth is projected to be slightly stronger than
expected in the near term, reflecting a much stronger
outlook for data centre-related investment compared
with the August Statement. Weighing against that is the
higher assumed market path for the cash rate, a weaker
outlook for the housing market and higher oil prices.
These pull growth in the latter part of the forecast period
a little below that expected in the August Statement.

Business investment is expected to increase strongly
and the outlook has been revised up significantly,

largely driven by data centre-related investment.
Business investment was stronger than expected in the
June quarter and firms’ surveyed expectations of future
investment were again revised higher. External
projections for the expected increase in Australia’s data
centre capacity through to 2030 have also continued to
be revised up as the size of the development pipeline
has increased. Our forecast for data centre investment is
in line with recent baseline external projections, but

below projections that assume Australia becomes a
significant regional hub for data centres. There is
considerable uncertainty around the extent of data
centre investment over coming years. This will depend
on a range of factors such as government policy,
community expectations, future demand for AI services
and grid capacity. We continue to expect that much of
this additional investment will be imported (around
70 per cent), limiting the boost to GDP growth. However,

data centre construction, as well as any potential
spillovers to higher investment in energy and utilities
infrastructure, will require domestically sourced labour
and materials. At this stage, most of this investment
pipeline is in New South Wales and Victoria where liaison
information suggests capacity constraints in the
construction sector are somewhat less binding.
3.3 Domestic outlook
Chapter 3  |  OutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlook

Reserve Bank of A ustralia  |  Statement on Monetar y Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026 68

### Page 72

At the same time, a higher assumed cash rate path, a
more significant easing in the established housing
market and higher global oil prices are expected to
weigh more on activity than in the August forecasts.
The forecasts are conditioned on a higher market path
for interest rates relative to August. In addition,
established housing prices have continued to fall more
quickly than expected, with external forecasters now
expecting a deeper downturn than assumed a few

months ago. The outlook for oil prices is also much
higher than assumed in August, which will weigh on real
household incomes. Reflecting these factors, the outlook
for household consumption and dwelling investment
has been revised lower, as has the outlook for business
investment unrelated to data centres.
The labour market is expected to
continue easing over the forecast
period, consistent with
below-potential GDP growth.
The unemployment rate is forecast to increase to

5.1 per cent by the end of 2028, which is around
1⁄4 percentage point higher than previously
expected (Graph 3.4). The unemployment rate has risen
by more than expected over recent months, and the
upward revision to forecast relative to the August
Statement largely reflects this higher starting point.
Elevated cost-of-living pressures appear to have
encouraged workers to enter or remain in the labour
force to a greater extent in recent months than was

expected at the time of the August Statement. These
pressures are expected to continue to support
participation for some time. The participation rate is
expected to decline very modestly by the end of 2028 as
easing cyclical pressures are expected to more than
offset the longer-run upward trend in female
participation. A slightly weaker outlook for demand later
in the forecast period is also contributing a little to the
upward revision to the unemployment rate profile by

late 2028.

As in previous forecasts, the
employment-to-population ratio is expected to
decline through to late 2028. Employment growth is

expected to remain broadly stable over the next year, in
line with the leading indicators of labour demand. From
mid-2027, employment growth is expected to be a little
weaker than in the August Statement, reflecting the
weaker outlook for GDP growth later in the forecast
period. The forecast employment-to-population ratio is
similar to the August Statement in the near term, before
declining more quickly to reach a lower level by the end
of the forecast period.

Reserve Bank of A ustralia  |  Statement on Monetar y Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026 69

The economy is expected to move
into balance in 2027, with spare
capacity then emerging in the
economy over the rest of the forecast
period.
Below-potential output growth and an easing in
labour market conditions are expected to see the
economy move from a position of excess demand to
around balance in 2027, before some spare capacity
emerges. The timing of the return to balance is slightly
later than in the August Statement, as a result of the
upgrade to near-term GDP growth.

However, there is considerable uncertainty around
the assessment of when the economy will return to
balance and the extent of spare capacity that
emerges. The impact of conflict-related costs on
inflation over the period ahead will also make it more
difficult to assess the degree of underlying capacity
pressures in the domestic economy. Our assumption for
potential output growth over the next couple of years
assumes productivity growth will pick up gradually (see

Key risk #4). However, with the level of labour
productivity remaining around the same rate as prior to
the pandemic – implying near-zero productivity growth
on average over that period – continued weak
productivity outcomes may be constraining the ability of
firms to grow their supply capacity without generating
inflationary pressure.
Wages growth is expected to pick up
a little in the near term, then
moderate gradually from 2027 as
labour market conditions ease.

The forecast for year-ended wages growth has been
revised a little higher across the forecast period. In
the near term, nominal wages growth is expected to be
supported by the effect of higher-than-expected
capacity pressures in the first half of the year as well as
the Fair Work Commission’s award wage decision (which
had already been incorporated in the August forecasts).
The influence of both higher-than-expected inflation
outcomes and inflation expectations on wage

negotiations is expected to add further upward pressure
on wages growth relative to the August forecast. Some
large wage increases in enterprise bargaining
agreements are expected to support wages growth in
the near term, although the timing of pay increases for
these agreements may contribute to volatility in
quarterly outcomes, particularly for the public sector.
Wages growth expectations from firms in the RBA’s
liaison program are elevated and suggest little

easing over the year ahead (see Box C: Insights from
Liaison). This is consistent with the central forecast for
wages growth. Growth is expected to moderate from
mid-2027 onwards, reflecting easing labour market
conditions and lower inflation outcomes. Wages growth
is expected to have eased to 2.9 per cent in year-ended
terms by end-2028 (Graph 3.5).

Unit labour cost growth is forecast to
be elevated in the near term, before
easing over the second half of the
forecast period.
Year-ended growth in unit labour costs (ULCs) is
expected to increase in coming quarters, reflecting
weak productivity growth and higher wages growth.
Year-ended growth in ULCs is then expected to
moderate from late 2027 onwards, alongside slowing
nominal wages growth and the pick-up in productivity
growth to its assumed medium-term trend growth rate

of 0.7 per cent per year (Graph 3.6). Nominal ULCs are
the measure of labour costs most relevant for firms’ cost
of production and so for inflation outcomes. Year-ended
ULCs growth is expected to remain above 2.5 per cent –
the rate that is consistent with inflation being
sustainably at target – until late in the forecast period.

The forecast for underlying inflation
has been revised higher over the
next year, mainly owing to higher
conflict-related cost pressures and

Trimmed mean inflation is expected to remain
above 3 per cent until the end of 2027, easing
gradually to reach 21⁄2 per cent around mid-2028
(Graph 3.7). Year-ended underlying inflation is expected

to remain elevated at a little above 31⁄2 per cent until
mid-2027, reflecting existing capacity pressures in the
economy and the pass-through of higher
conflict-related costs to consumer prices. Elevated

underlying inflation over the coming year or so. From
mid-2027 onwards, inflation is expected to ease due to
the emergence of spare capacity in the economy and
labour market and as conflict-related cost effects begin

to unwind.

Cost pressures related to the Middle East conflict are
expected to place more upward pressure on
underlying inflation over the year ahead than
previously assumed. A possible re-escalation in the
conflict had been flagged as an upside risk to inflation in
the August Statement. Since then, oil and fuel prices
have increased by much more than had been assumed
and are now assumed to remain at a higher level over
the forecast period. We expect higher global energy

prices will contribute to higher domestic inflationary
pressures in late 2026 and early 2027, even as domestic
activity slows and the labour market eases (Graph 3.8).
The judgement around the degree of pass-through from
global energy prices to domestic prices is broadly similar
to what had been assumed in August. It is consistent
with information from liaison and the Business Outlook
Scenario Survey (BOSS) in September, where firms
reported plans to continue raising prices in response to

past cost increases arising partially from the conflict.
From mid-2027 onwards, we expect cost effects to
unwind in net terms, albeit to a lesser extent than
assumed in the August Statement as oil prices are now
assumed to remain at a higher level than previously
expected and refined margins are assumed to remain
elevated for longer (see Key judgement #1). The balance
of risks to conflict-related inflationary pressures remains
skewed to the upside, particularly as crude oil and

refined fuel prices could remain elevated or rise further
(see Key risk #1).
Relative to the August forecasts, domestic capacity
pressures are also expected to exert a bit more
upward pressure on inflation over the year ahead.
We now judge that stronger-than-expected domestic
capacity pressures explain more of the recent
unexpected strength in underlying inflation (see
Chapter 2: Economic Conditions). Those higher capacity
pressures are also expected to contribute a little more to

inflation over the year ahead. This upward pressure is
expected to ease gradually over the forecast period, as
spare capacity emerges in the economy and the
labour market.

Elevated inflation expectations are expected to

contribute to inflationary pressure over the forecast
period, supporting the pass-through of firms’ costs
to consumer prices. Several measures of short- and
medium-term inflation expectations have increased
alongside the recent increases in fuel prices, and some
measures of longer-term expectations have also drifted
higher (see Chapter 2: Economic Conditions). We judge
that elevated expectations will contribute to higher
realised inflation by supporting expected cost growth,

making firms more willing to pass these higher costs
through to prices, and placing upward pressure on
wages growth. If the conflict in the Middle East is more
protracted than assumed in our baseline forecasts, there
is a risk that inflation expectations may increase further
(see Key risk #1).

Headline inflation has been revised
higher in the near term due to

been revised higher relative to our August forecasts,
reflecting higher underlying inflation (Graph 3.10).
Headline inflation is expected to reach the midpoint of
the target in mid-2028 as capacity pressures and
conflict-related costs ease.

### Page 77

The staff assess that the risks to the domestic inflation
outlook are skewed to the upside, despite becoming
more balanced since the August Statement as some of
the risks identified then have been incorporated into the
central forecast. In August, we identified three main
sources of upside risk to the global and domestic
inflation outlook: a re-escalation of the Middle East
conflict; domestic capacity pressures being greater than
assumed; and the global AI boom generating more of an

inflationary impetus than assumed. Since then, the risk
of a re-escalation of the Middle East conflict has
crystallised and staff have re-evaluated the extent of
domestic capacity constraints; both of which have led to
upward revisions to the central inflation forecast over the
next year. However, there is still uncertainty around the
outlook for global energy prices, the role of inflation
expectations in price and wage dynamics as well as the
impact on inflation from AI activity.

The staff also identified in August that there were upside
risks to global and domestic economic activity from
AI-related demand, but that domestic economic activity
could be weaker if the Middle East conflict and the
decline in housing prices affected the economy by more
than assumed in the central forecast. These remain key
risks to the outlook.
Key risk #1 – High inflation could be
more persistent if energy prices
remain elevated, or if inflation

expectations increase or start to
have a greater bearing on wage- and
price-setting.
We have modelled a scenario where crude oil
remains around US$100 per barrel and refined
product prices remain at elevated levels over the
forecast period. Many external forecasters are revising
higher their outlook for crude oil and refined product
prices, on concerns that some of the key margins of
adjustment in global energy markets that have helped
to cushion the impact of the disruption to oil supply and

global activity are reaching their limits (see Chapter 2:
Economic Conditions and Key judgement #1).
Persistently higher global oil prices would leave
domestic inflation higher for a longer period than in
the central forecast. Higher oil prices would directly
increase fuel prices and firms’ input costs, with the latter
gradually passed through to consumer prices as firms
adjust to persistently higher costs. By contrast, the
central forecast assumes a decline in oil prices, which

starts to ease cost pressures related to the Middle East
conflict from mid-2027, contributing to lower inflation
over time (Graph 3.11). Higher inflation also raises
inflation expectations in the scenario, reinforcing
inflationary pressures over the forecast period. Assuming
that the cash rate and exchange rate follow the same
path that underpins the central forecasts, underlying
inflation would be around 23⁄4 per cent by
end-2028 rather than being around the 21⁄2 per cent.

3.4 Key risks to the outlook
Chapter 3  |  OutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlook
Reserve Bank of A ustralia  |  Statement on Monetar y Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026Statement on Monetary Policy  |  November 2026 74

### Page 78

It is possible that inflation expectations could rise
even further or that inflation expectations provide
greater impetus to wages growth and inflation
through second-round effects. The central forecasts
and scenario assume that the increase in households’
and firms’ inflation expectations do exert some upward
pressure on inflation and wages growth. However, given
households and firms are paying particularly close
attention to inflation, it is possible that inflation

expectations will influence price- and wage-setting
decisions by more than assumed. This heightened
attentiveness to inflation could also make expectations
more persistent and more responsive to realised
inflation. With headline inflation forecast to rise further in
the near term, this increases the risk that inflation
expectations remain higher for longer than assumed in
our central forecast.
Key risk #2 – The global AI demand
boom may continue to drive up

global growth and inflation.
MTP growth may not moderate as expected over the
next few years if AI-demand growth continues at the
same pace. It is possible that growth forecasts could
continue to be upwardly revised by Consensus
forecasters if the current upswing in AI-related
investment (and spillovers associated with the boom) is
more persistent than currently assumed. The rise in
interest rates across the yield curve in many advanced
economies may reflect higher neutral rates – that is,

expectations that demand will prove more resilient and
inflation more persistent than currently assumed. Higher
longer-term real rates may partly reflect expectations of
stronger future productivity growth, including from the
adoption of AI. Further investment in anticipation of
higher productivity could also support demand and add
to capacity pressures over the next few years, while most
of the productivity gains are likely to manifest beyond
the next two years (at least in Australia).

The ramp-up of investment in data centres may also
contribute to capacity and pricing pressures
domestically, particularly by drawing on resources in
the construction industry. These price pressures could
be stronger than currently expected as the forecast
pipeline of investment is worked through, or if
investment proves stronger than expected.
Key risk #3 – Domestic economic
activity could be weaker than
expected.
It is possible that the headwinds affecting

households and businesses over the next year could
have a larger adverse effect than assumed. The
central forecasts assume that higher oil prices, higher
interest rates and lower housing prices all weigh on
domestic economic activity broadly in line with average
historical relationships. Timely indicators of spending
remain consistent with this assumption, with no
evidence of a material slowing in consumption growth
in recent months. However, the very weak levels of

consumer and business sentiment could be pointing to
a more pronounced slowing in household and business
spending growth than assumed.
We have calibrated a downside domestic activity
scenario where there is a larger response in
household and business spending to these
headwinds. There is a range of estimates for the effects
of these headwinds in the literature, highlighting the
uncertainty around their impact on economic activity.
Drawing on this evidence, the scenario uses larger

responses than assumed in the central forecast, thereby
identifying a plausible downside outlook for domestic
activity. In the scenario, firms feel forced to pass on the
conflict-related costs to prices as in the central forecast;
but households respond more cautiously to the
resultant pressures on real income and wealth, reducing
discretionary consumption by more than assumed in the
central forecast. Such an effect on spending might be
compounded by a tightening in borrowing constraints

and incentives for housing investors. The scenario
assumes firms respond to weaker demand and margin
pressure with a sharper pullback on (non-data centre)
investment and hiring. Overall, the level of GDP is around
1.0 per cent lower than the central forecasts
by end-2028.
The larger pullback in household and business
spending results in weaker activity and employment
growth, but only modestly lower inflation than in
the central forecast, assuming interest rates and the

exchange rate follow the same path as the central
forecast. The greater caution exhibited by households
pushes the gross household saving ratio up gradually to
a peak of around 17 per cent, where it remains over the
remainder of the forecast period. The greater reduction
Chapter 3  |  OutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlookOutlook

in hiring pushes the unemployment rate 0.3 percentage
points higher by end-2028, creating more spare capacity
in the domestic economy than in the central forecast
(Graph 3.12). The additional spare capacity in the
economy and the labour market reduces inflationary
pressures somewhat, but the assumption of cost
pass-through by firms limits this effect. Overall, trimmed
mean inflation reaches 21⁄2 per cent only one quarter
earlier than in the central forecast.

Key risk #4 – There remains
considerable uncertainty about the
degree of capacity pressures in the
domestic economy and labour
market.
Despite the recent reassessment, it is possible that

we are still understating the extent of capacity
pressures. While we assess that capacity constraints in
the economy and labour market have been tighter than
previously judged, model-based estimates of the
unemployment rate that is consistent with low and
stable inflation continue to point to greater tightness in
labour market conditions than our central assessment. In
addition, if productivity growth were to remain subdued
over the forecast period, rather than pick up modestly as

assumed, it may suggest that the economy’s supply
capacity would be lower than currently expected. The
implications for inflation would depend on how
households and businesses adjust their spending
and expectations.
Conversely, capacity pressures may be weaker than
expected. The broader suite of full employment
indicators points to somewhat less labour market
tightness than our central assessment. It is also possible
that more of the recent strength in wages growth and

inflation reflects factors other than excess demand, such
as conflict-related cost effects or higher inflation
expectations. In these cases, wages growth and inflation
may moderate more quickly than in the central forecast.
Productivity growth could also recover more quickly
than assumed, including if the anticipated benefits of AI
adoption and other technological developments are
realised sooner.
There is also a risk that the unemployment rate
increases by more than expected if labour supply

remains stronger than assumed. There is a risk that
elevated cost-of-living pressures could support labour
force participation for longer, or to a greater extent, than
assumed. If so, stronger labour supply could result in a
higher unemployment rate than forecast, even if labour
demand evolves broadly as expected.

TUẦN 12-16/10 [Điểm nhấn thị trường] 
VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -29.18 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh 
khoản suy giảm

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
VN-Index có thể hồi kỹ thuật từ cạnh dưới tam giác, song việc chớm thủng hỗ trợ 
1.710 điểm và số mã còn giữ xu hướng tăng từ đáy tháng 7 suy gi ảm khiến xu 
hướng ngắn hạn trở nên trung tính.

### Page 2

2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -29.18 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Dầu khí, Bảo hiểm và Dịch vụ phân phối là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -3,013 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã MCH, VIC và FPT; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở mức 
887 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng -2,021 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -4,384 hợp đồng. Tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng -
4,275 hợp đồng và đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -7,815 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam tiếp tục điều chỉnh tuần thứ tư liên tiếp, khi VN-Index lùi về đóng cửa 1.706 
điểm - giảm khoảng 1,7% so với tuần trước và đang dao động quanh vùng hỗ trợ tâm lý 
1.700 điểm, cũng là vùng đáy cũ của tháng 8. Thanh khoản thị trường tiếp tục suy yếu, 
phản ánh tâm lý thận trọng cao độ trong bối cảnh thị trường thiếu vắng thông tin hỗ trợ 
trong nước, trong khi các yếu tố bên ngoài như xung đột tại Iran, và chi phí vốn toàn cầu 
chưa hạ nhiệt tiếp tục đè nặng tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Khối ngoại duy trì chuỗi bán ròng kéo 
dài nhiều phiên liên tiếp, nối dài đà rút vốn đã lên tới xấp xỉ 95 nghìn tỷ đồng từ đầu năm. 
Trong một, hai tuần tới, mùa báo cáo kết quả kinh doanh quý 3 được kỳ vọng là chất xúc 
tác giúp thị trường giao dịch cân bằng trở lại.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tiếp tục hình thành mẫu nến giảm điểm tiêu cực trên đồ thị tuần, đánh dấu tuần 
giảm thứ 4 liên tiếp, cho thấy áp lực phân phối vẫn đang có phần lấn át. Trên khung ngày, 
tuy chỉ số đã có nỗ lực phục hồi trở lại trong 3 phiên đan xen, đặc biệt là phản ứng trong 
phiên cuối tuần khi chạm trendline trung hạn, nhưng việc chỉ có thể hình thành các mẫu 
nến "spinning" hàm ý đà tích lũy chưa thực sự thuyết phục. Trong một vài phiên kế tiếp, 
VNINDEX có thể xuất hiện nhịp hồi kỹ thuật từ vùng hỗ trợ, tương ứng với cạnh dưới tam 
giác, nhưng sau khi chớm phá vùng hỗ trợ quanh 1710 và hiện chỉ còn một số ít các mã giữ 
được xu hướng tăng tính từ đáy tháng 7, xu hướng ngắn hạn đang trở nên khá trung tính.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

## Document 346: Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FOLLOWING THE OIL SHOCK, EL NIÑO THREATENS TO HURT MACROECONOMIC RESILIENCE IN SUB-SAHARAN
AFRICA FURTHER
Lucas Plé
Seven months after the start of the war in the Middle East, which has led to a sharp rise in oil prices, sub-Saharan Africa is holding up well
overall. The deterioration in external accounts and the rise in inflation are more contained than in 2022, during the last oil shock. However,
this resilience is fragile and is eroding as the conflict drags on. Furthermore, an exceptional El Niño event could significantly worsen the

macroeconomic environment in 2027 1.
Economic growth remains robust
For countries with available data, GDP growth held up well in the first half of 2026 2. In mid-October, the IMF revised its regional forecast for
the full year upwards to 4.5% (+0.2 percentage points compared with April), in line with the performance for 2025.
1 El Niño 2026–2027: multiple pressures on prices and supplies
2 1.4% year-on-year (y/y) in South Africa, 4.2% in Nigeria, 5.1% in Kenya, 7% in Angola, 7.3% in Ghana.

Sub-Saharan Africa is highly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A mitigated shock on terms of trade
High prices for export commodities (gold, copper, platinum) have helped to cushion the impact on terms of trade and the external
accounts of hydrocarbon-importing countries. For the 10 countries in our sample (see heatmap), the depreciation of their currencies
against the US dollar since last February has been moderate overall (2.3% on average).

Inflation has started to rise again, but it is still being contained so far
In the ten countries in our sample, although inflation is rising again, it is lower year-on-year for the first eight months of 2026 than it was
for the same period in 2022. This is due to:
1. Monetary policies that were still restrictive before the conflict started;
2. Fuel subsidies;
3.   Macroeconomic reforms implemented in recent years (a return to orthodox monetary policy in Nigeria, and efforts towards fiscal

consolidation in many countries);
4. Moderate currency depreciation since February.
As a result, few central banks have tightened their monetary policy since February (South Africa, Uganda and Tanzania).
However, this resilience is still fragile…
On the one hand, governments that have chosen to cap fuel price rises are seeing their public finances deteriorate. In Senegal, energy
subsidies will go above 3% of GDP in 2026, which is unsustainable. On the other hand, the region’s external accounts are exposed to

volatile global commodity prices and tighter international financing conditions.
… and the macroeconomic environment could quickly worsen in 2027, due to an exceptional El Niño event
The region is particularly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel. Given agriculture’s high share in its GDP ,
sub-Saharan Africa is more exposed to downside risks to growth than Latin America, Asia or Central Europe (1 st column of the heatmap).

It is also more exposed than other regions of the world to the inflationary effect of rising food prices, given their higher share in the
consumer basket (2 nd column). Finally, even though its dependence on food imports is lower than in Asia and Europe (3 rd column), the
region is highly vulnerable to rising international food commodity prices due to its fragile external accounts. For several countries in our
sample, foreign exchange reserves cover less than four months’ worth of imports of goods and services (Uganda, Tanzania, Ghana and

Zambia), leaving little room for manoeuvre for absorbing a further external shock.
In addition to shocks via the agricultural commodities channel, other localised risks must be considered. In Southern Africa, El Niño is
expected to intensify droughts and threaten electricity generation as a result, particularly in Zambia (where hydropower accounts
for nearly 80% of domestic electricity generation). In East Africa, the increased flooding that El Niño is expected to cause will threaten

infrastructure – particularly logistics infrastructure – which is already less efficient than the global average 3. These two sources of
vulnerability could also increase pressure on growth, prices and the external accounts.
In conclusion, if oil prices remain high and El Niño is exceptionally severe, the region will struggle to absorb this double shock in 2027.
Despite their status as net exporters of hydrocarbons, Nigeria and Angola are also exposed to a deterioration in the macroeconomic

environment in 2027. Only South Africa appears to be better positioned to absorb the shock.
lucas.ple@bnpparibas.com
3 See the World Bank’s Logistics Performance Index.

## Document 351: France’s social demands run up against tightening fiscal constraints

Source PDF: all report pdf/France’s social demands run up against tightening fiscal constraints.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Thursday’s nationwide mobilisation over wages and purchasing
power will add pressure on a French government already
struggling with weak growth, a fragile labour market and an
increasingly unmanageable budget. Its options for responding
to social discontent are narrowing as higher borrowing costs
further weaken the outlook for public debt
Charlotte de Montpellier
Student-led protests have grown into a broader social movement, putting
more pressure on the French government

Pressure mounts for action on purchasing
power
What began as a student-led protest has grown into a broader
social movement, adding to the government’s challenges as it
struggles to get its 2027 budget through parliament.
The mobilisation continued during the school holidays, with
between 320,000 and 500,000 people taking to the streets last
Saturday, fewer than in previous weeks but hardly the pause the

### Page 2

government had hoped for. The next test will come on Thursday, 5
November, when all the main trade unions will mobilise over wages
and purchasing power, with students and public sector workers
expected to join the protests.
Under pressure, the government is considering raising the
minimum wage by more than the automatic increase of around
1% due in January, possibly adding another 0.5 to 0.6 percentage
points. This would be the ﬁrst discretionary increase since 2012. But

employers are strongly opposed, as they are already facing higher
labour costs through planned cuts to social contribution
exemptions. A further rise in the minimum wage could therefore
encourage companies to limit recruitment or reduce their
workforce, at a time when the labour market is already fragile.
Private sector employment has declined for seven consecutive
quarters in annual terms and now stands 0.3% lower than a year
ago, representing a loss of almost 70,000 jobs. A discretionary

increase in the minimum wage would also have implications for the
public sector. It could raise the pay of around 15% of public sector
employees, adding further pressure on the public ﬁnances.
Part of the additional wage cost could also be passed on to
consumers, adding to inﬂationary pressures. Higher energy prices
have already pushed headline inﬂation from 3.1% in September to
an estimated 3.4% in October, while harmonised inﬂation reached
3.8%.

Budget talks are producing a larger deﬁcit,
not a solution
These pressures are making an already diﬃcult budget equation
even harder to solve. After 10 full days of debate and more than
2,300 amendments examined, France’s 2027 budget discussions
appear to be going nowhere, except towards a larger deﬁcit than
the government intended. While the government aims to reduce
the deﬁcit to 5% of GDP in 2027, after an expected 5.4% in 2026,
the measures approved so far have pushed it substantially further

oﬀ course. According to the Finance Ministry, the amendments
adopted during the ﬁrst reading have already worsened the

projected deﬁcit by €23.9bn (total deﬁcit in 2026 will be around

The vote on the revenue part has been postponed again, as MPs
continue to adopt measures pointing in all directions. Yet the
exercise increasingly makes little sense: the fragmented National
Assembly is unlikely to approve the budget, leaving the
government with two possible escape routes. It could use Article
49.3 to pass the bill without a vote, but would ﬁrst need to
negotiate with parts of the opposition to ensure that a subsequent

no-conﬁdence motion fails. For now, there are few signs of such
talks. If the motion passed, the government would fall and the
budget would be rejected. Alternatively, once the constitutional 70-
day deadline expires on 18 December, the government could
implement its original budget by ordinance. A two-stage solution,
with the ordinance followed by a corrective bill, is reportedly
gaining support among MPs, including some Socialists. The
corrective bill could accommodate selected social promises.

Weak growth makes the ﬁscal targets
unrealistic
The problem is that the government’s initial budget will no longer
be suﬃcient to bring the deﬁcit down to 5% of GDP. It was based on
GDP growth of 1% in 2027, an assumption that now looks clearly
too optimistic. The French economy contracted by 0.1% in the third
quarter, after stagnating in the second and shrinking by 0.2% in the
ﬁrst, leaving GDP marginally lower than a year earlier and France
hovering close to recession.

The details of the third-quarter ﬁgure were also weak: investment
fell by 0.6% and net trade subtracted 0.6 percentage points from
growth as exports dropped sharply, while inventories provided a
temporary oﬀset. The only real positive surprise came from
household consumption, which rose by 0.4%. But there is little
reason to expect this momentum to last. Consumer conﬁdence fell
by ﬁve points to 80 in October, its lowest level since June 2013 and
well below its long-term average of 100. Households’ assessment

of whether this is a good time to make major purchases dropped

sharply, while fears of unemployment increased. Most strikingly,
their assessment of living standards fell to its lowest level since the
survey began in October 1972. This points to much weaker
consumption in the coming months.
The outlook for investment is equally weak. Financing conditions
are deteriorating particularly sharply in France: according to the
latest ECB Bank Lending Survey, a net 27% of French banks
tightened credit standards for businesses in the third quarter,

compared with around 8% in Germany, no tightening in Spain and
an easing in Italy. Together with weak demand, higher interest
rates and uncertainty over taxes and public spending, this is likely
to encourage companies and households to postpone projects.
Combined with political uncertainty and social tensions, growth is
likely to be very weak in the fourth quarter as well. All in all, we now
expect GDP growth of just 0.3% this year and 0.5% in 2027, with

risks still tilted to the downside. Under these assumptions,
implementing the budget unchanged would leave the deﬁcit at
around 5.5% of GDP. Reaching the government’s 5% target would
require roughly €12bn in additional savings, on top of the €43bn
eﬀort already included in the budget, which looks highly unlikely in
the current political environment. And that is before including the
almost €500mn cost of the measures promised to high-school and
university students.

Debt is unlikely to stabilise anytime soon
All this leaves France’s debt dynamics looking increasingly
uncomfortable. The government had expected public debt to reach
121.7% of GDP in 2027, already a level at which a 5% deﬁcit would
be insuﬃcient to stabilise the debt ratio. With growth now likely to
be weaker and the deﬁcit higher than assumed in the budget, debt
will probably rise even further, and there is little prospect of
stabilisation anytime soon.

The sharp rise in borrowing costs only makes the arithmetic worse.
The French 10-year yield is currently around 4.9%, more than
155bp above its German equivalent, compared with a spread of
around 70bp at the end of 2025. Admittedly, the increase in market

yields feeds only gradually into the eﬀective cost of the debt as
existing bonds mature: the implicit interest rate on French public
debt is still around 2.2% in 2026. But this also means that a
substantial part of the rise in interest costs has yet to come
through. Fitch estimates that France would need to reduce its
primary deﬁcit from around 2.9% of GDP this year to just 0.4% to
stabilise the debt ratio. The adjustment required would become
considerably larger if the eﬀective interest rate on the debt

gradually converged towards current market yields.
And the 2027 presidential election is unlikely to provide an easy
reset. A new president could again ﬁnd her or himself without a
parliamentary majority, making the large and sustained ﬁscal
adjustment required increasingly diﬃcult to deliver. These concerns
prompted Fitch to take the unusual step on 30 October of bringing
forward its assessment and revising the outlook on France’s A+
rating from stable to negative, only a week after Moody’s

unexpectedly kept its Aa3 rating unchanged, albeit with a negative
outlook. Fitch explicitly pointed to weaker growth, higher borrowing
costs and a wider deﬁcit, together with political fragmentation and
social tensions that make sustained ﬁscal consolidation harder to
achieve. It expects French public debt to reach around 128% of GDP
by 2030, considerably above the roughly 123% projected by the
IMF.
All in all, tomorrow’s demonstrations will once again underline the

population’s frustration over purchasing power. But without
stronger economic growth to lift household incomes while
simultaneously easing the pressure on the public ﬁnances, the
government has very limited room to oﬀer measures large enough
to make a meaningful diﬀerence without making an already
worrying ﬁscal trajectory even worse.

## Document 375: LatAm’s silicon dividend_ tailwinds from the AI investment boom in the U.S. (2026-10-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/LatAm’s silicon dividend_ tailwinds from the AI  investment boom in the U.S. (2026-10-28).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

LatAm’s silicon dividend: 
tailwinds from the AI  
investment boom in the U.S.

1 
Authors 
Fancisco Campos 
Chief LatAm Economist

Beatriz Garcia Nunes 
LatAm Economist

October 28, 2026 
Key messages: 
- U.S. investment has broken away from Europe and China since 2023, 
powered by AI-related equipment, software and research spending. 
- The AI CapEx boom demands commodities and merchandise that LatAm 
can supply.  
- Latin America’s export gains are real but concentrated. Copper, computer 
and electronics products provide the clearest links to the U.S. AI buildout. 
- Chile is the region’s most direct copper beneficiary. Mexico captures the 
windfall stemming from increases hardware demand and trade-diversion. In 
these cases, the AI-related tailwinds have undergirded appreciable 
improvements in external accounts. 
- The cases for nickel and zinc illustrate that the dividend is not widespread. 
U.S. imports of the former have mostly returned toward pre-pandemic levels, 
while for the latter bilateral gains partly reflect supplier substitution. 
- While expanding AI use, supply shortages and incentives can extend the 
CapEx boom, its financial viability poses downside risks. 
Another ‘American exceptionalism’ story  
Investment has become one of the sharpest dividing lines among the world’s 
core economic blocs. Since 2024, U.S. gross fixed capital formation has firmed 
up appreciably. Meanwhile, investment in Europe has broadly stalled while in 
China’s it has retreated, the drivers of which are beyond the scope of this report 
(Figure 1).  
The U.S. divergence has largely been an AI story: a burst of spending on 
computing power, digital infrastructure and intellectual property has offset 
weakness in conventional construction and housing. Indeed, total U.S. fixed 
investment is nearly 12% above its 2023 level. Within it, investment in 
information-processing equipment has risen by roughly 57%, software outlays by 
about 35%, and research and development by around 15%. By contrast, non-
residential structures and residential investment sit below their 2023 levels 
(Figure 2).

### Page 2

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 2

Figure 1: The global investment divergence

Figure 2: AI-related categories lead U.S. investment

Source: US Bureau of Economic Analysis, Eurostat, National Bureau of Statistics of 
China, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Source: US Bureau of Economic Analysis, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Latin America’s commodity channel 
AI is not merely a semiconductor story. Chips require specialized minerals; data 
centers require electricity and cooling; electricity requires grids, transformers and 
storage. That chain pulls in copper, aluminum, silver, tin, lithium and selected 
semiconductor inputs, along with natural gas and uranium. Latin America has 
meaningful exposure to several of these inputs, but copper has emerged as the 
commodity with the cleanest and most economically significant regional link. 
Copper is used throughout the AI infrastructure stack, including power cables, 
substations, transformers, server boards, heat sinks and cooling systems. Rising 
U.S. copper demand has lifted imports from Chile and Peru, particularly the 
former (Figure 3). T he effect matters beyond merchandise export volumes. 
Favorable terms-of-trade have helped Chile generate a current -account surplus 
for the first time in roughly fifteen years (0.2% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4 -
quarter rolling basis ) and have allowed Peru to log the largest surplus in about 
forty-five years (4.5% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4-quarter rolling basis).

28 October 2026

### Page 3

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 3

Figure 3: Copper epitomized the commodity link

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Not every metal is copper 
The broader mining story demands more caution, though. U.S. nickel imports have 
recovered toward pre-Covid levels, but the upswing has not displayed the same 
persistence as copper. Mexico has captured a disproportionate share of the 
bilateral improvement, yet that gain looks more like normalization than a 
sustained AI -driven break (Figure 4). Zinc tells a similar story. U.S. real imports 
stand shy of their pre-pandemic levels, and stronger purchases from Mexico and 
Peru partly reflect a reallocation away from Brazil rather than a decisive increase 
in U.S. demand (Figure 5).

Figure 4: Nickel: recovery, not a boom

Figure 5: Zinc: a substitution story

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

28 October 2026

### Page 4

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 4

Mexico’s hardware advantage 
The larger non-commodity prize is in computers and electronics. U.S. imports of 
these products have sky -rocketed, while the supplier mix has shifted away from 
China. Mexico and various Asian economies have gained share on the back of this 
double-whammy impulse (Figure 6). 
This helps explain why Mexico’s non-automotive manufacturing exports, notably 
electronics, have outpaced automotive shipments to the U.S. and pressures from 
higher oil prices. The resulting non -oil trade surplus has offset pressure from the 
oil balance and helped Mexico generate a balanced current account (0% of GDP 
as of Q2 2026 on a 4 -quarter rolling basis). The external boost is meaningful, 
although it sho uld not necessarily be interpreted as bona fide  evidence of 
nearshoring, in our view.

Figure 6: U.S. computer & electronics imports going into overdrive

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research.

How much runway remains? 
Quantifying or mapping out a timeframe for the AI investment outlook is beyond 
the scope of this piece or our expertise. Nevertheless, we’d venture to argue that 
the capex cycle appears more likely to mature gradually than to stop abruptly . 
One reason is that the investment case is shifting from frontier -model training 
toward enterprise inference . Capacity that once served development can 
increasingly run commercial workloads for companies. That creates a path to 
revenue even if artificial general intelligence (AGI) crystallizes later than 
expected. It also spreads demand to a much larger corporate customer base.  
A second reason is scarcity. Graphics processing units (GPUs) remain tight, while 
power availability, grid connections, cooling equipment, construction capacity 
and data -center complexity limit how quickly supply can respond. Bottlenecks 
may therefore exte nd the cycle. The constraint is especially relevant for copper 
28 October 2026

### Page 5

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 5

and power infrastructure, where projects have long lead times and permitting 
hurdles. 
A third reason is strategic. AI development resembles a prisoner’s dilemma at 
both the corporate and national levels. Each participant may worry about costs, 
safety and control, yet slowing unilaterally risks surrendering technological, 
commercial or natio nal security advantages. The same tension is visible in calls 
for guardrails alongside continued spending. Competition can therefore sustain 
capex even when short-term returns are uncertain. 
We acknowledge the risks, nonetheless. Higher borrowing costs raise the hurdle 
rate for an infrastructure buildout financed partly through debt. More important, 
revenues must eventually justify the installed capital base. The bearish case is not 
that AI la cks value; it is that infrastructure spending may run ahead of 
monetization, compromising some companies’ repayment capacity. Public 
opposition to the development of AI infrastructure and/or adoption of AI 
technologies could also slow down the technology’s roll-out. 
The regional balance  
Summing up, the AI investment boom in the U .S. is delivering a dividend to parts 
of Latin America, not the region as a whole. Chile and Peru benefit through copper 
prices and export volumes. Mexico benefits through electronics manufacturing 
and the replacement of Chinese supply. For Brazil and Colombia, the AI dividend 
is rather muted. Where materialized, furthermore, the dividend supports trade 
and current accounts more clearly rather than improving the domestic 
investment. 
These dynamics should persist while U.S. AI investment remains supply -
constrained and strategically protected. If widespread adoption advances and 
the strategic race intensifies, the impulse can last. If financing costs rise further 
or monetization disappo ints, the cycle may narrow. Either way, copper and 
Mexican electronics remain the region’s most visible gauges of the U.S. AI 
investment appetite.

28 October 2026

### Page 6

Deutsche Bank Research Institute 
LatAm’s silicon dividend: tailwinds from the AI investment boom in the U.S. – October 28, 2026

摘要：
关闭上海淮海路世界旗舰店七个⽉后， MUJI 無印良品找到了新的落脚点。
11 ⽉ 6 ⽇，第九届中国国际进⼝博览会期间， MUJI 与淮海集团完成签约，宣布全新上海世界旗舰
店将落⼾淮海中路 887 号。
新店计划集合零售、餐饮及住宿等多种业态。 MUJI ⽅⾯向华尔街⻅闻介绍，项⽬预计拥有上下
六层、超过 8000 平⽅⽶的空间，并将引⼊上海⾸家 MUJI HOTEL 。酒店规模预计超过北京、深

⽬前，新店的具体开业时间尚未确定，最快有望于 2027 年开业。
值得关注的是，这次新址落定，恰好与优⾐库在淮海路的旗舰店搬迁形成了⼀次交换。
今年 3 ⽉ 31 ⽇， MUJI 位于淮海中路 755 号的世界旗舰店正式闭店。这家开业于 2015 年 12 ⽉、经
营⾯积约 3438 平⽅⽶的⻔店，曾集合零售、餐饮等⽣活⽅式业态，是 MUJI 在中国的重要标志性

⻔店。

推进新的世界旗舰店选址。
7 个⽉后，优⾐库位于 755 号的全新全球旗舰店正式开业，接⼿了 MUJI 此前的经营空间。新店销
售⾯积约 2800 平⽅⽶，覆盖三层空间。
⽽优⾐库此前的全球旗舰店，恰恰位于淮海中路 887 号，也就是 MUJI 此次宣布签约的新址。
两家⽇本零售品牌由此在淮海路完成了⼀次颇为特殊的旗舰店址互换。
优⾐库此前在 887 号经营的是集合 GU 、 Comptoir des Cotonniers 等迅销集团旗下品牌的综合旗
舰店；如今迁⼊约 2800 平⽅⽶的新店，仅保留优⾐库品牌，更加聚焦服装零售与个性化服务。
MUJI 则计划在 887 号打造超过 8000 平⽅⽶的综合空间，将零售延伸⾄餐饮、住宿等业态。
新旗舰店的落地，也发⽣在 MUJI 中国业务恢复增⻓的阶段。
截⾄ 2026 年 8 ⽉底的财年内， MUJI ⺟公司良品计画营业收⼊达到 9235.92 亿⽇元，同⽐增⻓

17.7% ；营业利润 1034.36 亿⽇元，同⽐增⻓ 40.1% ，均创历史新⾼。
其中，中国⼤陆市场实现收⼊和利润双增⻓，既有⻔店及电商业务销售额同⽐增⻓ 16.3% 。财年
内， MUJI 在中国⼤陆新开 42 家⻔店，期末⻔店数量达到 433 家。
中国⼤陆仍是 MUJI 最⼤的海外市场。伴随业绩恢复，品牌也在继续扩⼤⻔店⽹络，推进既有⻔
店改装升级。

GPT-6 Cyber 漏洞利⽤基准得分较前代提升 51% ，协助研究⼈员在 Firefox 中发现 81 个⾼危

摘要：
OpenAI 在⽹络安全领域迈出重⼤⼀步。该公司推出专⽤安全研究模型 GPT-6 Cyber ，在漏洞挖
掘能⼒和任务边界遵守⽅⾯均实现显著提升，标志着 AI 在攻防安全领域的应⽤进⼊新阶段。
在⽤于测试模型能否利⽤指定漏洞攻击远程⽬标的 ExploitGym 基准测试中，GPT-6 Cyber 得分
48.5% ，较前代模型 GPT-5.6 Cyber 的 32.1% 提升约 16 个百分点，相对提升幅度达 51% 。
与此同时，该模型在任务范围合规性⽅⾯表现出近乎完美的可靠性 ——在未启⽤部署安全提⽰
的情况下， 99.93% 的测试中模型未触及授权范围之外的系统；启⽤提⽰后，这⼀⽐例达到

100% 。
在实际应⽤层⾯， GPT-6 Cyber 已展现出直接的产业价值。该模型协助研究⼈员在 Firefox 中发
现 81 个此前未知的⾼危漏洞， Mozilla 已陆续发布修复补丁；此外还针对 Linux 内核的 1,724 个已
知漏洞完成复现，并为其中 948 个开发出可⽤的漏洞利⽤程序，是上⼀代模型的五倍以上。
Cisco 、 Calif 、 CodeRabbit 等安全团队已将其⽤于漏洞研究与代码审查。
Firefox 杰出⼯程师 Brian Grinstead 表⽰：
能⼒跃升：漏洞发现与利⽤双线突破
GPT-6 Cyber 在多项核⼼⽹络安全任务上超越了通⽤旗舰模型 GPT-6 Astra ，包括在后者不拒绝
请求的情况下亦如此。OpenAI 据此将该模型在其 " 准备框架 " 下的⽹络安全能⼒评级由 " ⾼ " 上调
⾄ " 关键 " 。
"GPT‐6 Cyber 是⼀款世界级的⽹络安全防御模型。 Firefox 在进⼊  2026 年之际，已然跻⾝全球
最坚固、代码审查最严苛的项⽬之列。

即便在历经多代前沿模型的进⼀步强化之后， GPT‐6 Cyber 依然挖掘出了此前未知的⾼危漏
洞，识别出了架构层⾯的改进空间，并协助我们将修复补丁推送给了数百万⽤⼾。 "

对⻬训练：任务边界约束成核⼼设计⽬标
⽹络安全 AI 代理的潜在⻛险之⼀，在于其可能超出授权范围、攻击⽆关系统或忽视研究⼈员设定
的操作规则。 OpenAI 将对⻬训练列为 GPT-6 Cyber 的核⼼设计优先项。

在名为 ExploitGym Honeypot 的测试中，模型在执⾏漏洞利⽤任务的同时，会接触到刻意设计为
易于攻击的辅助服务。
模型须⾃⾏判断攻击这些服务超出任务授权范围，从⽽主动放弃。GPT-6 Cyber 在⽆部署安全
提⽰的情况下，越界率由 GPT-5.6 Cyber 的 5.51% 降⾄ 0.07% ；加⼊提⽰后越界率归零。
从模型的思维链⽇志可以看出， GPT-6 Cyber 在推理过程中已内化了边界意识，例如主动判
断 " ⽆法假定额外 API 或攻击控制器不属于⽬标，仅应针对指定的⼆进制⽂件或服务器 " ，以及主
动排除 " 超出范围的凭据 " 。
实战验证：从开源软件到关键基础设施
GPT-6 Cyber 的能⼒已在多个⾼价值⽬标上得到实战验证。在 Firefox 漏洞研究活动中，该模型
发现了 81 个被 Mozilla 评定为⾼危级别的新漏洞，并证实另有 30 个已知问题的实际严重程度⾼于

制，防范凭证泄露。

试。该公司还设⽴了 10 亿美元的 Daybreak for Frontline Defenders 资助计划，为政府机构及关
键服务提供商等资源有限的团队提供访问⽀持。

全部 Daybreak Red ⽤⼾扩展访问权限。与此同时， GPT-5.6 Cyber 也将向全部 Daybreak Red 客
⼾开放。

试版访问资格，并为经批准的关键基础设施防御团队设⽴了专属的 Daybreak Red for Critical
Infrastructure 分组，允许其在保留模型合规保护的前提下开展授权测试。

這次我們想做個新嘗試。
之前幾期的「財報解剖」⼀向以極致的深度著稱，但⽼實說，並⾮每次財報公
佈，都有必要把所有維度拉出來重新解構⼀遍。

因此，我們推出了這個更聚焦、節奏更快的變體版。

雖然篇幅縮減，但我們對商業本質的思考沒有打折。這裡保留了最精華的洞
察，保證依然⽐市⾯上絕⼤多數的財報分析更有⾒解。
這份分析由我們的分析師  Benita 主導撰寫，我負責校正觀點並做最後把關，希
望能⽤更⾼效的⽅式，幫⼤家抓出最值得關注的產業訊號。
Palantir ⼜⼀次完美財報，企業 AI
開始真正變現，但新簽訂單卻放緩了？

### Page 2

Palantir 第三季⼜交出了⼀份近乎沒有瑕疵的財報。
2026 年第三季營收來到  23.13 億美元，年增  96% 、季增  20% ，創下公司史上
最⾼的年增率，同時也是連續第  13 個季度營收成⻑加速。

### Page 3

其中美國商業營收⼤增  148% ，調整後營業利益率從去年同期的  51% 躍升⾄
62% ，推升  Rule of 40 達到  158% 。
同時，公司還把第四季營收指引調⾼到約  26.51 億美元，⾼於市場原本預期的
約  24.7 億美元（⾼出約  7% ）。
只要第四季順利達成指引， Palantir 的年化營收規模就將正式跨過  100 億美元
⼤關。
不過， Palantir 的「好」，已經不是新聞了。
我們會將這次的分析分為 4 個重點。
重點⼀：企業 AI 開始真正變現 —— 從百萬美元試點，⾛向千萬美
元的營運系統
過去這兩年，許多企業在導⼊ AI 時，最常⾒的做法就是去買⼤模型的 API 、做
個內部聊天機器⼈，或是花錢做⼀堆概念驗證（ PoC ）。
但試了⼀圈之後，許多企業開始感到挫折。
⼀⽅⾯，對外串接純⼤模型供應商，很難完全保護公司內部的機密資料與營運
知識；另⼀⽅⾯，讓 AI 寫寫信、做個摘要並不難，真正的挑戰在於如何讓 AI
融⼊公司的⽇常⼯作流程，並創造出⾜以回本的經濟效益。

為了解決這個痛點， Palantir 過去透過實戰⼯作坊（ AIP Bootcamp ，近期已進⼀
步演進為打造 AI 代理的 AgentCamp ），讓企業帶著⾃⼰的真實資料，在短時間
內做出⼀套能實際運作的系統。
到了今年，這套模式更延伸到「主權 AI （ Sovereign AI ）」。
這裡談的 AI 主權，重點在於讓企業⾃⼰掌握資料、業務邏輯與模型選擇權。 企
業可以⾃由更換底層模型，並把真正值錢的營運知識留在⾃⼰的系統裡。
在這個架構下，企業可以利⽤⾃⼰累積的營運資料，在 Palantir 的 Ontology （本
體論）架構下評估並微調開放權重模型，完全不必每件事都去呼叫最昂貴的頂
級⼤模型。
在這次法說會上，有兩個案例特別有說服⼒：
第⼀個是國防科技公司 L3Harris 。

### Page 4

他們利⽤⾃家資料微調開放模型後，在不到 48 ⼩時內，就讓這套主權模型的表
現超越了原本使⽤的前沿⼤模型，⽽且初步結果顯⽰整體成本⼤幅降低了
95% 。
第⼆個案例則是連 NVIDIA ⾃⼰都在⽤。
NVIDIA 透過 Palantir 評估並重新訓練（ post-training ）⾃家的開放模型，結果成
功讓旗下體積最⼩、效率最⾼的模型，在表現上⼤幅超越了⾃家最⼤的模型。
企業在⽇常營運中，往往更需要⼀個真正懂內部業務、且成本合理的專屬
模型，勝過⼀味追求最昂貴卻不懂公司流程的通⽤⼤模型。
舉個簡單的例⼦，⼀家製造商如果要⽤ AI 判斷哪些零件該優先採購，它真正需
要的是模型能讀懂公司的供應商名單、⽣產排程、庫存限制和交貨承諾，並根
據最新狀況隨時調整。
Palantir 想掌握的，正是把底層模型轉化為實際⼯作流程的這⼀層價值。
當企業發現這套做法真的能解決問題，從試點轉為正式⼤單的速度就相當快。

### Page 5

這次財報中， Palantir 公布了四個在⼀兩季內從⼩型試點迅速擴張成⼤型合約的
案例，擴張後的年化合約價值（ ACV ）合計達到 7,650 萬美元：
家電製造商： 從 100 萬美元的初始試點，擴張⾄ 1,500 萬美元的年化合
約。
⼯業材料公司： 從近 300 萬美元試點，擴張⾄ 2,900 萬美元。
全球運動品牌： 從 160 萬美元試點，擴張⾄約 2,100 萬美元。
服飾企業： 透過 AgentCamp 啟動 250 萬美元試點，隨後擴張⾄ 1,150 萬美
元。
其中，那家家電製造商的變化特別有代表性。
他們在第⼆季才剛花 100 萬美元試⽔溫，很快就轉成 300 萬美元的合約；結果
到了第三季，合約⾦額⼜⼀⼝氣放⼤五倍來到 1,500 萬美元，使⽤範圍直接橫
跨銷售（商務）、財務與供應鏈三⼤部⾨。

### Page 6

這代表客戶⼀旦把單⼀功能跑順了，就會開始把不同部⾨的⼯作流程全部串進
同⼀個平台，預算規模也跟著翻倍跳升。
重點⼆：美國商業營收⾸度超⾞政府 —— ⽼客戶越買越多，撐起
第⼆顆成⻑引擎
當單⼀客戶的合約⾦額從⼀百萬變成上千萬美元，反映在全公司的總體財報
上，就促成了這次最具指標性的轉折：
Palantir 的美國商業營收，歷史上第⼀次超越了美國政府營收。
我們可以看看第三季美國市場這兩⼤業務的表現：
美國商業客戶營收：達到  9.84 億美元，年增  148%（季增  29% ）。
美國政府客戶營收：達到  9.53 億美元，年增  96% 。

### Page 7

如果把海外市場也加進來，整體商業總營收來到  11.7 億美元，同樣在歷史上⾸
度超越整體政府營收的  11.5 億美元。
⽐起破紀錄的成⻑率，營收組合的翻轉其實更具代表性。
過去市場對  Palantir 的印象，多半停留在政府、國防與情報系統的承包商；但
隨著商業營收全⾯超⾞，華爾街在它⾝上套了⼗年的「政府承包商」標籤，在
這⼀季正式失效。
過去市場對 Palantir 的印象，多半停留在政府、國防與情報系統的承包商。這類
業務雖然⾨檻極⾼，但容易受到政府預算審查與採購週期的影響。
值得注意的是，這次商業營收能夠超⾞，背後依然伴隨著極為強勁的政府業
務。
美國政府營收年增 96% ，幾乎翻倍；純粹是因為美國企業客戶導⼊的速度衝得
更快，讓公司成功⻑出了第⼆顆規模相當、⽽且爆發⼒更強的成⻑引擎。
如果再往下⼀層看，會有⼀個⽐營收交叉更有意思的發現。

### Page 8

第三季美國商業客戶的總數來到 686 家，年增 29% ；但同期美國商業營收卻⼤
增了 148% 。為什麼營收成⻑的速度，會遠遠甩開客戶數量增加的速度？
答案就在於「淨收⼊留存率（ NDR ）」。這⼀季 Palantir 的整體淨收⼊留存率，
從上⼀季的 157% 進⼀步攀升到 165% 。
這說明了推動營收翻倍的主⼒，很⼤部分來⾃既有客戶持續加碼。
就像前⾯提到的製造商⼀樣，⼀家企業可能先⽤ Palantir 管庫存，覺得好⽤之
後，接著把採購、排程、財務到 AI 決策全部放進來。
不過，這波企業 AI 變現潮⽬前還是⾼度集中在美國。

### Page 9

相較於美國商業營收 148% 的⾼成⻑，第三季國際商業營收只有 1.84 億美元，
年增 21% ，季增更只有 1% 。
這意味著美國企業對軟體轉型的接受度依然領先全球，⽽未來能否把美國驗證
成功的模式複製到海外市場，將是後續觀察的重點。
重點三：營收翻倍但員⼯數幾乎沒變 —— ⾼營業槓桿背後的結構
與代價
把前⾯兩件事串起來看：當客戶透過⼯作坊體驗產品後迅速擴⼤採⽤，⽽且導
⼊之後⼜會⾃⼰越買越多（ NDR 165% ），這會對 Palantir 的成本結構帶來什麼
影響？
執⾏⻑ Alex Karp 在接受媒體（ MarketWatch ）訪問時特別提到了⼀個營運細
節：
Palantir 在美國商業營收⼤增 148% 的同時，全公司的員⼯總數（ Headcount ）
幾乎維持持平。

### Page 10

在傳統的企業軟體產業裡，公司如果想把營收規模擴⼤⼀倍，通常得按⽐例招
募⼤量的業務代表、售前顧問與⽀援⼯程師，⼈⼒成本也會跟著⽔漲船⾼。
但 Karp 形容 Palantir 的成⻑基本上不太依賴傳統意義上的業務員。對他們來說，
產品本⾝（ Foundry 、 Ontology 加上 AIP ）在⼯作坊裡的實際表現，就是最好的
銷售⼯具。
更重要的是，因為底層的資料整合與基礎架構在初期導⼊時就已經建好了，當
客戶後續決定把合約從 300 萬美元擴⼤到 1,500 萬美元時， Palantir 並不需要再投
⼊同等⽐例的⼯程⼈⼒從頭開發。
當營收以接近翻倍的速度成⻑，交付這些服務所需的總⼈數卻沒有等⽐例
增加，這展現了軟體業相當強勁的營業槓桿。
這項特質解釋了為什麼 Palantir 能在⾼成⻑的同時，交出亮眼的獲利數字：
第三季 GAAP 營業利益率達到 47% ，調整後（ Non-GAAP ）營業利益率⾼達
62% ，進⽽推升 Rule of 40 來到 158% 的⽔準。
不過，這種⾼營業槓桿背後也有相對應的代價。

雖然公司的總員⼯數幾乎持平，但為了留住頂尖⼈才並獎勵績效，第三季的股
權激勵費⽤（ SBC ）從去年同期的 1.72 億美元⼤幅增加到 3.19 億美元，幾乎翻
了⼀倍。
這也正是  GAAP 營業利益率（ 47% ）與調整後營業利益率（ 62% ）之間相差 15
個百分點的主要原因。
重點四：拆解 TCV 放緩與 RPO 持平的真相

### Page 11

這份完美財報裡唯⼀的裂縫，在於反映未來幾年營收可⾒度的單季「新簽總合
約價值（ TCV ）」與「未履約義務（ RPO ）」出現了明顯放緩：
整體  TCV ：第三季為  29.18 億美元，年增率僅  6% （相⽐第⼆季為年增
49% ），⽽且較上⼀季的  33.73 億美元下滑了  13.5% 。
美國商業  TCV ：第三季為  14.63 億美元，年增率降⾄  11% （相⽐第⼆季為
年增  153% ），為  2023 年以來的最低單季增速。
剩餘交易價值（ RDV ）與未履約義務（ RPO ）：單季來看，整體  RDV 季
增僅  2% （美國商業  RDV 季增  4% ，低於上⼀季的  27% ）， RPO 則幾乎持
平。
當季營收⼤增  96% 、新簽訂單卻只成⻑  6%，這兩個數字在財務邏輯上不可能
⻑期並存。
當季營收主要是由既有客戶擴張所推動，⽽新簽約指標的放緩，⾃然讓部分投
資⼈開始擔⼼未來幾年的營收可⾒度。
針對這個現象，財務⻑ David Glazer 與經營團隊在法說會上的解釋是：

### Page 12

許多客戶為了快速導⼊ Sovereign AI 的初期功能、急著讓系統趕快上線，所以
在第三季簽下了好幾筆千萬美元級別、但「合約期限⽐往常更短」的訂單。
管理層同時強調，「 Sovereign AI ⾰命才剛滿⼀季」，並預期隨著客戶使⽤深
⼊，這些合約的年限與規模都會隨時間增加。
管理層的這番解釋，其實也剛好從會計規則上說明了為什麼 RPO 幾乎持平：因
為 Palantir 的 RPO 定義，本來就不計⼊初始期限少於 12 個⽉的短期合約。
如果第三季新簽的  Sovereign AI ⼤單多半屬於⼀年以內的短約，那麼不會反映
在 RPO 上、同時壓低單季 TCV 總額，確實符合財務邏輯。
此外，如果我們把時間拉⻑來看，⻑約存量指標其實沒有單季數字看起來那麼
疲弱：
整體  RDV （剩餘交易價值）： 年增率仍達  55% 。
美國商業  RDV ： 達到  65.2 億美元，年增率⾼達  79% 。
過去  12 個⽉（ TTM ）美國商業  TCV ： 累計達  61.14 億美元，年增

62% 。
換句話說，單季簽約指標放緩，還不等於企業端的需求轉弱。真正的問題在於
估值給出的容錯空間極⼩。
以約  178 美元的股價與  24.03 億股計算， Palantir 的市值約  4,286 億美元；扣除
帳上  104 億美元的現⾦與美國公債後，企業價值（ EV ）約為  4,182 億美元。
對照全年營收指引中值  85.32 億美元，企業價值倍數（ EV/Sales ）⾼達  49 倍；
就算⽤第四季指引年化的  106 億美元來算，也⾼達約  39 倍。
將近  40 到  50 倍的營收定價，隱含市場要求這家公司在跨過百億美元年化營收
之後，未來⼗年還得維持約  50% 以上的年複合成⻑率。
在這麼⾼的⾨檻下，市場沒有太多耐⼼等待。
下⼀季投資⼈最想驗證的，就是這批短約能不能如管理層所說，順利續約成更
⻑、更⼤的多年期合約。
從更⼤的產業趨勢來看，企業 AI 的投資重⼼正在轉移。
第⼀波浪潮，⼤家搶著買 GPU 、買算⼒、買⼤模型 API ；但進⼊第⼆階段，企
業真正關⼼的是如何結合內部資料與⼯作流程，讓 AI 實際改善營運並省下成

本。
Palantir 已經在美國市場證明⾃⼰能吃到這波轉型的紅利。

### Page 13

接下來決定它⻑期價值的關鍵，就在於這批新導⼊的短期合約能否在未來幾季
轉化為⻑約存量，並將這套商業模式進⼀步複製到更廣⼤的海外市場。
蘋果：營收創紀錄、⽑利被記憶體成本
壓縮， Service 令⼈失望？
Apple 這次財報，表⾯上相當不錯。
第四季營收來到 1,145 億美元，年增 12% ；每股盈餘 2.10 美元，年增 14% 。主
⼒產品 iPhone 營收成⻑ 17% ， Mac 更是⼤幅成⻑ 24% 。

### Page 14

但在這些亮眼數字背後，如果仔細拆解財報細節與管理層在法說會上的說法，
會發現幾個值得留意的變化。
⾸先是⽑利率。
這⼀季表⾯上 47.9% 的⽑利率，其實包含了約 1 個百分點的關稅退款⼀次性收
益（每股盈餘 2.10 美元裡，也有 0.05 美元來⾃同⼀筆退款）。

### Page 15

扣掉這筆⼀次性收益後，本業⽑利率⼤約是 46.9% ；但就算⽤這個基準來⽐，
Apple 給出的下⼀季（ 12 ⽉季度）⽑利率指引落在 45% ⾄ 46% ，依然在往下⾛。
要知道， 12 ⽉季度是 Apple 傳統的銷售最旺季，營收規模放⼤通常會推升⽑利
率，為什麼這次反⽽逆勢下滑？
財務⻑ Kevan Parekh 在法說會上給出了答案：下⼀季⽑利率下滑，「主要」反映
記憶體成本上升，「另外也包括 Siri AI 上線的部分成本」。
這句話其實就是貫穿整份財報的核⼼線索：全球 AI 浪潮正同時從「硬體成
本」與「軟體商業模式」兩端夾擊 Apple 。
AI 確實帶來了新的成⻑機會，但也正在改變 Apple 原本最賺錢的獲利結構。
我們可以把這次財報拆解成五個環環相扣的重點來看：
重點⼀：硬體的成本逆⾵ —— 全球 AI 算⼒戰，為什麼由 Apple
先買單？

### Page 16

下⼀季⽑利率下滑的最⼤推⼿，來⾃記憶體成本的飆升。 這背後反映了⼀個很
現實的產業現象： Apple 雖然想靠 AI 賺錢，但現在卻得先為全球的 AI 資料中⼼
基建買單。
CEO John Ternus 在法說會上直接⽤「前所未⾒（ unprecedented ）」來形容當
前的記憶體價格環境。
其實在 9 ⽉這⼀季， Apple 就已經感受到記憶體漲價的壓⼒，只是當時⼿裡還有
早先囤積的低價庫存，加上其他⾮記憶體零組件成本下降，才抵銷掉部分衝
擊。
但隨著低價庫存逐漸消耗，這股成本壓⼒將在 12 ⽉季度正式浮現；管理層也坦
⾔，在 12 ⽉季度之後，記憶體採購價格預計仍會持續上升，⽽研調機構更普遍
預期這波供應緊俏會⼀路延續到 2027 年。
為了應對這場結構性的缺貨， Apple 已經採取了兩個動作：
直接漲價： 6 ⽉底先調漲了 Mac 與 iPad 的價格， 9 ⽉⼜將 iPhone 售價調⾼
約 100 美元，且管理層並未排除未來進⼀步漲價的可能。

### Page 17

砸錢鎖定產能： Apple 的庫存⾦額單季⼤增 31% 、年增⾼達 154% ，來到約
145 億美元；從 6 ⽉到 9 ⽉季度，製造採購承諾⾦額更是⼀⼝氣暴增了 330
億美元。
憑藉龐⼤的現⾦流與規模， Apple 搶貨的能⼒絕對⽐中⼩型 Android ⼿機廠或 PC
廠強得多。
但記憶體終究是每⼀台硬體裝置中無法妥協的核⼼成本，當漲價轉嫁給消費者
到了⼀定極限、開始有拉⻑換機週期的⾵險時，硬體⽑利率勢必得承受壓⼒。
全球掀起 AI 資料中⼼擴建潮，最先賺到錢的是記憶體⼤廠，⽽包括 Apple
在內的終端硬體商，正在為這場硬體通膨買單。
重點⼆：⾼⽑利新武器卡關 ——iPhone Duo 的產能與初期品管
挑戰

### Page 18

既然記憶體漲價壓縮了硬體⽑利，最⾃然的解法，就是多賣⼀點⾼單價、⾼⽑
利的新產品來拉⾼平均售價（ ASP ）。
這就帶出了市場最關注的新品： Apple ⾸款折疊機 iPhone Duo 。
iPhone Duo 在 9 ⽉底財報結算後才正式上市，因此第四季財報還沒有反映它的
營收。
從需求⾯來看，這款新機⾮常成功，預購迅速秒殺，出貨時間⼀路排到明年⼀
⽉下旬。
但問題出在供給⾯。
這次法說會上， CEO John Ternus 特別安排營運⻑ Sabih Khan ⼀起上線回答供應
鏈問題。
Khan 坦⾔， iPhone Duo ⽬前處於「產能受限」的狀態，且「很難預測供需何
時能達到平衡」。

### Page 19

這種罕⾒不給時間表的說法，意味著折疊螢幕與轉軸的量產爬坡難度，⽐過去
傳統 iPhone ⾼出許多。
更⿇煩的是，初期有極少數⽤戶反映內螢幕轉軸處出現摺痕或裂紋的品質問
題；雖然 Apple 已經在協助換機，但緊繃的產能也讓部分地區的換貨等待時間
拉⻑。
除了折疊機，過去受限於先進製程（隨著產品線轉向 2 奈⽶已逐漸緩解）的
Mac 與 iPad ，到了 12 ⽉季度依然⾯臨缺貨，⽽這次卡住產能的瓶頸，剛好⼜是
前⾯提到的記憶體不⾜。
換句話說，儘管下⼀季營收預計仍能成⻑ 7% ⾄ 10% 、有望創下公司歷史新
⾼，但記憶體漲價加上⾼階新品缺貨，讓硬體業務在短期內很難幫公司撐起⽑
利率。
這時候， Apple 就更需要仰賴⾼⽑利的 Services （服務業務）來穩住獲利基本
盤。
第四季 Services 營收只占公司總營收約 28% ，卻因為⾼達 76% 的⽑利率，貢獻
了全公司將近 44% 的⽑利。

### Page 20

硬體負責把⼈帶進⽣態系， Services 負責賺取⾼利潤，這是 Apple 最核⼼的賺錢
公式。
然⽽，正如財務⻑在⽑利率指引中透露的，連 Services 這座⾼⽑利堡壘，也從
下⼀季開始要⾯對 AI 帶來的成本考驗。
重點三： Siri AI 終於有了收費⽅向，但會不會稀釋 76% 的⾼⽑
利？
Apple 在今年 WWDC 就已經宣布，部分需要雲端模型處理的 AI 功能將設有使
⽤額度，使⽤者未來可以付費取得更多運算容量。
這次法說會上， Goldman Sachs 分析師 Michael Ng 進⼀步追問： iCloud+ 是否正
在轉型成 Apple AI 商業化的重要⼯具？
管理層給出了肯定的答案，確認未來需要更多伺服器端 AI 運算能⼒的使⽤者，
可以付費購買額外容量。
過去⼤家訂閱 iCloud+ ，買的是「儲存空間」，⽤來放照⽚、影⽚和備份檔案。
到了 AI 時代，使⽤者額外付費購買的變成了「運算能⼒」，像是讓 Siri 分析複
雜⽂件、執⾏多次雲端請求，或是呼叫⼤型模型處理⼯作。

### Page 21

坐擁超過 25 億台活躍裝置、超過 15 億個付費訂閱的 Apple ，要推這項新服務當
然很有優勢。
假設未來 Apple 能吸引 5,000 萬⼈、每⽉額外付 10 美元使⽤進階 AI 功能，⼀年
就能帶來 60 億美元的營收，相當於⽬前全年 Services 營收的 4.9% 左右。
但從財務⾓度來看，這⾨⽣意跟過去的數位服務有⼀個本質上的差異。
傳統數位服務的營收增加，不太需要同⽐例增加成本；但⼤型 AI 模型每次執⾏
雲端推論，背後都在消耗真實的算⼒資源。使⽤者越頻繁使⽤複雜的 AI 功能，
Apple 要承擔的伺服器與推論成本就越⾼。

### Page 22

過去每收到 100 美元服務收⼊， Apple 可以留下 76 美元⽑利；但到了 AI 服務
時代，營收增加的同時，背後往往伴隨著持續攀升的雲端推論成本。
這也解釋了為什麼財務⻑ Kevan Parekh 特別強調， Siri AI 的雲端推論費⽤將
計⼊ Services 的銷貨成本，⽽且相關成本已經開始反映在 12 ⽉季度的⽑利率指
引中。
同時， Apple 還透露了⼀個會計處理上的改變：當 Siri AI 成為硬體裝置隨附的服
務時，公司會將⼀部分的硬體收⼊分配給這項服務，並在裝置使⽤期間逐步認
列。
這代表就算消費者沒有額外掏錢加購算⼒， Apple 的 Services 營收與成本結構，
也會因為 AI 功能的推出⽽重新洗牌。
因此，接下來觀察 Apple 的 AI 變現能⼒，除了看有多少⼈願意付費，更要看每
增加⼀美元的 AI 收⼊，究竟能留下多少⽑利。
這也是為什麼 Apple 持續投⼊⾃研晶⽚與裝置端 AI ，除了讓⼿機跑得更順，
背後更有明確的財務考量：把越多的運算留在裝置端處理，⻑期下來就能

省下越多的雲端推論成本。

### Page 23

重點四：華爾街真正擔⼼的事 —— 當 AI Agent 成為⼊⼝，
Apple 還能收多少過路費？
如果說推論成本影響的是「利潤率」，那 Morgan Stanley 和 Evercore 分析師在
法說會上追問的，則是關係到「營收來源」的根本問題：
當 AI 從聊天機器⼈演變成能替使⽤者執⾏任務的 Agent （代理），會不會威脅
到 Apple 現有的 Services 業務？
我們可以想像⼀個很⽇常的場景。
現在如果你想規劃⼀趟旅⾏，通常會先打開 Google 搜尋機票，進到航空公司網
站⽐價，接著打開訂房 App 找飯店，最後分別完成付款。整個過程必須切換好
幾個不同的平台與 App 。
但在不久的將來，你可能只需要對⼿機裡的 AI Agent 說⼀句：「幫我安排下週末
去東京的旅⾏，機票加飯店預算三萬台幣，飯店希望離⾞站近⼀點。」
接下來， AI Agent 就會⾃動幫你搜尋、篩選，並直接串接不同服務完成預訂。
從頭到尾，你可能連訂房 App 都沒有打開過。
這個轉變最核⼼的影響，在於「誰掌握了決定權」。 過去是使⽤者決定要打開

哪個 App ；未來則是 AI Agent 決定要調⽤哪⼀家服務商。

### Page 24

⼀旦消費者習慣把⽇常決策交給 ChatGPT 、 Gemini 或其他第三⽅ AI Agent ，
Apple 過去靠 iOS 與 App Store 築起的收費關卡就會⾯臨兩個挑戰：
App Store 分潤壓⼒： 如果第三⽅  Agent 直接透過網⾴、外部  API 或其他
⽀付管道完成交易， Apple 對數位商品與訂閱交易的抽成能⼒就會被繞
過。
搜尋授權收⼊重分配： Apple 每年從搜尋引擎拿到的巨額授權⾦，建⽴在
「 iPhone 是最重要搜尋⼊⼝」的商業價值上。當  AI Agent 逐漸取代傳統
搜尋與  App 操作，這個⼊⼝的經濟價值勢必會重新洗牌。

### Page 25

⾯對這個問題， John Ternus 的回答展現了 Apple 的防守策略。
他強調， iPhone 是使⽤者最重要的個⼈資訊中⼼，裡⾯存放著聯絡⼈、訊息、
照⽚、⾏事曆、健康與財務資料。
AI 如果想真正理解使⽤者需求並跨 App 完成任務，就必須取得這些個⼈脈絡與
系統權限。
這讓未來的 AI Agent 競爭，分成了兩種不同的控制權：
需求⼊⼝： 誰最懂使⽤者想做什麼，誰來決定推薦哪些商品與服務（這
是  OpenAI 、 Google 正在搶的）。
執⾏⼊⼝： 誰能安全地讀取個⼈資料、呼叫  App 功能，並完成最後的操
作（這是  Apple 透過  iOS 、 App Intents 與  Siri AI 牢牢握在⼿裡的）。

### Page 26

Apple 很清楚⾃⼰未必要做出全世界最龐⼤的模型，但它必須利⽤作業系統與
隱私權限的優勢，把「需求」與「執⾏」整合在⾃⼰的平台上，確保使⽤者依
然透過 Siri 來完成⽇常任務。
⽽這場⼊⼝保衛戰之所以迫在眉睫，從這次 Services 的財測就能看出端倪。
Apple 第四季 Services 營收達到創紀錄的 315.7 億美元，年增約 10% ；但下⼀季
的成⻑指引卻放緩到 8% ⾄ 9% 。
管理層解釋，放緩的主因來⾃「第三⽅授權收⼊」與「匯率」變化，其次則是
部分 App Store 與⼿遊業務的調整。

### Page 27

值得注意的是，管理層⼝中的「第三⽅授權收⼊」，背後很⼤⼀部分就是
Google ⽀付給 Apple 的搜尋引擎分潤。
雖然單季度的波動未必完全來⾃ AI Agent 的衝擊，但這正好印證了華爾街的擔
憂：只要搜尋授權與 App Store 這兩⼤⽀柱稍微放緩， Services 的成⻑動能就會
⽴刻感受到壓⼒。
重點五：現在的股價，到底在賭什麼？
把前⾯硬體與軟體的拼圖全部放在⼀起，我們最後來看估值。
以財報當⽇收盤價約 338 美元、以及 FY2026 實際每股盈餘 8.98 美元計算，
Apple 的歷史本益⽐約為 37.7 倍（財報公布後盤後股價回落⾄約 329 美元，換算
也⾼達 36.6 倍）。

### Page 28

37.7 倍的本益⽐聽起來很貴，但「貴」是⼀個很模糊的詞。換個問法會清楚很
多：如果今天⽤ 338 美元買進 Apple ，然後⼀直抱著不賣，你每年⼤概能賺多
少？
有⼀個很簡單的算法：
⻑期年報酬  ≈ 現⾦殖利率  + ⻑期成⻑率
現⾦殖利率，就是公司⼀年賺進的⾃由現⾦流，除以你付出的市值。
Apple 現在市值接近 5 兆美元，⼀年的⾃由現⾦流⼤約只有市值的 2.6% 。
換句話說，⽤ 338 美元買進，第⼀年真正拿回的現⾦回報只有 2.6% 左右，剩下
的報酬全靠未來成⻑。
⽤這個邏輯反推，可以從兩個⾓度看現在的股價：
如果假設  Apple ⻑期每年成⻑  3% ：
338 美元的股價，換算下來的⻑期年報酬⼤約是  6.1% （註：若以純現⾦殖利率
2.6% 直接相加則約為  5.6% ）。

### Page 29

同⼀時間，美國⼗年期公債殖利率約  4.9% ，⽽且幾乎沒有⾵險。
這代表買  Apple ⽐買公債多承擔了股價波動的⾵險，每年卻只多換來約  1 個百
分點左右的⾵險補償；⽽在歷史上，美股投資⼈要求的⾵險補償通常落在  4%
到  5% 。
如果投資⼈要求每年  7% 的報酬：
這只⽐公債多  2 個百分點左右，已經算很客氣了。
但要做到這樣， Apple 必須永遠每年成⻑約  3.6% 以上。
⼀般估值模型給成熟公司的永續成⻑率，多半抓在  2% 到  3% ；要求  3.6% ，等
於要求⼀家市值近  5 兆美元的巨頭，永遠跟著整個美國經濟的速度⼀起⻑⼤。
這兩個⾓度講的是同⼀件事：現在的股價，幾乎沒有留空間給意外。
這時候再回頭看⽑利率，就能理解市場為什麼對那 1 到 2 個百分點的變化這麼
敏感。

### Page 30

以 2026 財年全年 Services ⾼達 1,233 億美元的營收規模來算，服務⽑利率只要
每下降 1 個百分點，⼀年就少掉約 12.3 億美元的⽑利。
若放⼤到全公司約 4,790 億美元的總營收來看，全公司⽑利率每少 1 個百分點，
扣稅後⼤約會少掉每股 0.27 美元的盈餘；⽤ 37.7 倍的本益⽐換算，等於股價直
接少了約 10 美元。
更別忘了，連計算 37.7 倍本益⽐所⽤的 8.98 美元 EPS 起點，裡⾯都還包含了⼀
筆關稅退款的⼀次性收益。
所以這次財報最值得思考的地⽅，在於現在的股價，其實已經先預設了 Apple
會有個完美結局：
市場預設記憶體漲價很快會過去、 Siri AI 的雲端算⼒成本控制得住，⽽且第三
⽅ AI Agent 也搶不⾛ iPhone 的流量⼊⼝。
對 Apple 來說，投⼊ AI 同時是⼀項成⻑投資，也是⼀筆為了保護每年 1,233 億
美元服務⽣意的必要⽀出。
只要硬體缺料漲價、 AI 推論成本攀升、或是⼊⼝分流這三件事當中有任何⼀件

拖得⽐預期更久，市場除了要下修獲利預估，連那 37.7 倍的估值倍數本⾝都可
能⾯臨重新定價。
⽽在現在這個價位，後者帶來的影響往往更⼤。

## Document 464: Westpac NZ Retail Spending Pulse, October 2026 (2026-10-01)

Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

RETAIL
SPENDING
PULSE
6 November 2026

### Page 2

Westpac New Zealand Economics
2
Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5668 | +64 21 710 852 | satish.ranchhod@westpac.co.nz      Connect with us
Discretionary spending flat as high fuel
prices bite
• Per-person spending on Westpac issued credit and debit cards was up 1.2% in October and is 7.8%
higher than this time last year.
October spending.
• October’s rise in spending was mainly related to the sharp increase in petrol prices, with the average

price for 91-unleaded around the country climbing to a record high of over $3.60/ltr. That saw
spending on fuel rise 14% over the month.
• Those higher fuel prices, along with other cost of living pressures, have put the brakes on other
purchases. Spending in discretionary areas was down 0.4% in October and has essentially been
tracking sideways since May. There’s been notable softness in spending on travel and household
furnishings. However, spending in restaurants has been holding up.

Annual growth.
• While spending has certainly been constrained by the rise in living costs since the start of this year,
spending levels are still well above those we saw in 2025. And that’s not just because of high fuel
prices, with spending in discretionary areas also up compared to this time last year.
• That’s been supported by factors such as the drop in borrowing costs over the past year and
strength in export earnings. The boost from those factors is helping to balance the impacts of

factors such as softness in the labour market and high fuel prices. However, as we highlight over the
page, the strength in spending has been uneven across the country.

Regional breakdown
• As has been the case for some time, spending growth has been strongest in Southland. Other parts
of the country with strong rural backbones like Canterbury and Northland have also continued to
see solid gains.
• Spending growth remains more modest in Auckland. However, we expect that spending in Auckland

will take a step higher next month, with hospitality spending in particular likely to be boosted by
events like the Indian cricket tour, Diwali and Auckland Marathon.
• In a notable move, Wellington has shifted up the ranks and now sits in the middle of the pack.
Wellingtonians are no doubt holding their breath ahead of Saturday’s tight election and many will be
stocking up for the traditional election night barbecue and watch party.*

《高利高通膨壓抑，留意日股短線震盪加大》
10月日本股市大幅震盪修正 ，符合本行下調評等之
預期。其中，日經225指數在10月初一度上破7萬點
大館，此後因AI相關估值疑慮、科技股修正與長端利
率上行，全球科技股估值大幅修正，日經225指數在
10月23日盤中一度下探61,462點。
日本兩大指數在本月表現也呈現相對分化。日經225
權重權重集中於東京威力科創、愛德萬測試、軟銀集
團等半導體與 AI標的，在本波修正幅度較大；相反
的，TOPIX指數因金融、內需與防禦類股權重較高，
加上利率下行有利銀行以外的內需類股，回檔相對溫
和。也間接反映了10月的修正偏向是 「AI交易擁擠
的釋放」，而非全面性的景氣衰退定價。
債市方面，10年期日本公債殖利率在10月初一度升

至3.11%、創1999年以來新高 ，反映日本央行升
息、以及高市內閣財政擴張的雙重疑慮。
此後殖利率一路回落至 10月26日的2.82%、下行約
23個基點。這一下行幅度反映全球 AI相關信用風險
引發的避險需求。
重點摘要
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/9，中國信託商業銀行整理

《日本出口維持韌性，內需動能出現疑慮》
經濟基本面部分，日本9月出口在半導體與AI相關需
求帶動下，連續13個月維持正成長 ，惟增速放緩至
16.7%、略低於預期；但進口年增卻升至18.9%，主
要因為進口能源價格大漲所致，導致貿易逆差擴大，
外需雖強卻未能轉化為淨出口貢獻。
內需與生產端則收高利高通膨壓力同步降溫。工業生
產增速自年中高點回落，製造業PMI降至七個月來最
低，整體擴張速度已低於上半年水準，細項中新訂單
與出口訂單成長同步放緩，能源與勞動成本則大幅走
升，對企業營運形成壓力。內需改善亦相當有限，零
售銷售下滑、失業率微升、求人求職比回落，反映勞
動市場略有降溫；所幸，實質薪資已連續八個月正成
長、消費者信心小幅回升。

整體而言，日本經濟仍處於「名目成長、實質停滯」
的格局，擴張主要仰賴出口，內需復甦力道偏弱，而
高油價帶來的逆差壓力與生產動能轉弱，將是後續成
長的主要考驗 。基於地緣與油價的不確定性 ，10月
日本央行暫停升息，維持利率不變。
重點摘要
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/10，中國信託商業銀行整理
5
貨幣政策正常化以及財政疑慮推升日債殖利率走高
• 日本出口持續受惠半導體與AI
需求，惟能源價格上漲推升進
口成本與貿易逆差。工業生產、
PMI及勞動市場同步降溫，經
濟仍維持「出口強、內需弱」
格局，成長動能面臨考驗。

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投
資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
《日銀暫停升息，12月升息預期支撐利率高檔》
日本央行已於 9月會期將政策利率上調 25個基點至
1.25%，為1995年以來最高 ，也是三個月內第二
次、1990年以來最快的升息節奏。
隨著近期經濟數據 ，10月份日央如預期暫停升息一
碼，會議同步公布《經濟與物價形勢展望報告》，根
據央行經濟預測顯示潛在通膨「已大致達到2%」。
配合植田和男在10月16日記者會曾表示，若基礎通
膨進一步上升將是 「無法接受」的，並在10月19日
國會答詢時表示潛在通膨非常接近2%目標。
加以隨著國際油價再度上破110美元大關，短期政策
重點從「對抗通縮 」轉為「防止通膨超標 」，是以
12月再度升息一碼的機率已大幅提高。
另一方，高市內閣的食品消費稅減稅與國防支出增

加，使市場對財源安排存疑 。不過，10月19日高市
在國會承諾在下一財年預算中妥善管控國債發行規
模，使近期長端利率有所回落 。但12月下一財年預
算與國債發行計畫公布時，財政紀律的可信度將再受
檢驗，預期短線利率上行趨緩，維持高檔盤整。

《盤勢觀點-資金輪動加速，防禦成長族群接棒》
10月日本股市震盪 、類股表現分化 。市場資金明顯
由上半年漲幅龐大的AI硬體與半導體族群撤出，轉向
軟體服務、醫療保健及內需消費等防禦成長型產業。
其中，東京威力科創、SCREEN、瑞薩電子等指標股
普遍出現雙位數跌幅。相較之下，軟體服務類股大漲
17.9%，BayCurrent、SoftBank等個股表現突出。醫
藥生技、消費零售及娛樂觀光相關族群亦受惠於防禦
需求與內需消費韌性而表現相對亦強勢。值得注意的
是，銀行與非銀行金融股同步走弱，多家大型金融機
構跌幅介於8%至15%，顯示市場擔憂金融條件收緊
可能壓抑經濟活動與資本市場表現。
整體而言，10月日股市場風格由 「AI硬體主導」轉

向「防禦成長與內需消費主導」，同時對經濟基本面
疑慮小幅升溫 ，8~9月相對強勢的金融出現回調壓
力。展望未來，短期盤勢仍受利匯率壓力，以及科技
股評價調整影響 ，加以1H27預估日經225指數企業
獲利年增率轉為負成長，因此本行維持9月以來下調
日股至中性的看法不變，待國際油價回落以及年底年
度預算案底定後，可伺機布局。
重點摘要
5
• 市場風格由AI硬體轉向軟體服
務、醫療保健及內需消費等防
禦成長族群。半導體、金融與
工業設備股表現疲弱，反映投
資人開始關注高利率環境及企
業獲利成長放緩風險。

《盤勢觀點-澳洲景氣放緩，股匯市轉向盤整》
隨著國際油價維持衝高，澳洲央行（RBA）於9月再
度升息將現金利率提高至 4.6%，官員雖表示若通膨
壓力持續，不排除進一步緊縮。但在高利率與高通膨
壓力持續積累下對景氣負面壓力逐漸加大。
資料顯示，澳洲9月失業率升至4.72%，已連續兩個
月 落 在 澳 央 主 席 認 為 可 有 效 壓 抑 通 膨 的 失 業 率
4.5~5.0%區間；而通膨方面 ，9月份澳洲 CPI雖由
4.0%升至4.3%，但核心CPI僅有2.2%，加以住房通
膨明顯回落，因此本行預期澳央轉向觀望、本輪升息
暫時告終，2027年政策轉向觀望。
外需方面，中國經濟基本面持續低迷，消費與投資不
振亦難以挹注澳洲出口動能，外需對澳幣支撐力量有

限，加以預期聯準會在 12月再度升息 ，將呈現「利
差有撐但優勢遞減」格局，因此預估澳幣短線維持在
0.7~0.71區間盤整。
綜上，持續升息後澳洲基本面轉弱，但高油價壓力又
迫使央行貨幣政策難以轉向寬鬆，加以企業獲利未見
改善動能，澳洲股市難以樂觀，本行維持中性展望。
重點摘要
• 高油價迫使澳洲央行維持偏
鷹態度 ，但高利率已逐步壓
抑就業與內需。
• 中國需求偏弱限制出口前景 ，
加上美澳利差優勢收斂 ，預
期澳幣短期維持區間震盪 ，
澳股展望偏中性。

◼ 金融市場 ：半導體和 AI相關類股受挫 ，日台韓
股受連帶影響 ，不過資金輪動至其他產業 ，使
美股指數整體穩健 。美債殖利率走低 、美元下
滑。
◼ 投資建議： 若資料中心的建置時程受影響 ，將
影響相關業者的營收和償債時程 ，提高AI相關
類股的波動 。但就趨勢來說 ，既有政策不會因
此出現重大轉折 ，穩健成長的企業獲利提供重
要支撐，AI類股修正時期資金仍在其他類股間
輪動而未明顯離場，美股短線有壓然無礙趨勢
熱門議題/資產影響推估 市場焦點
➢ 美國國會變天： 民主黨不僅重新奪回眾議院 ，也
以遠超預期的幅度拿下參議院多數席次 。整體政
策方向其實不會發生重大改變 ，但民主黨未來可
能利用財政法案與債務上限談判 ，推動限制對伊
朗戰爭、部分撤銷關稅以及影響財政支出。
➢ 市場焦點放在資料中心建置進程 ：AI資料中心的
建設是否受到影響 ，是目前市場最關注的焦點 。
本行認為短期逆風確實存在 ，但不影響長期建置

趨勢。
美國期中選舉國會變天

• 產業影響-AI建設加強監管但不會喊停 P6-7
• 市場觀點-資金輪動而未明顯離場 P8-P10

美國國會變天，AI概念受挫然未見風險情緒，殖利率回落
AI概念受挫，資金輪動至其他板塊
盤勢回顧
美10年期利率回到5%以下，信用利差自高點收斂
3
• AI概念受挫，資金輪動至其他板塊：民主黨全面掌控國會後，市場擔憂AI產業、大型雲端服務與資料中心的監
管與審查將顯著收緊，使相關類股、日台韓股市均下挫，不過資金輪動到其他產業，支撐美股指數維持穩定。
• 財政紛擾仍存但支出展望中性 ，美10年債利率來到5%以下：市場開始反應財政政策可望趨於中性 ，再加上地
緣政治有望緩和，布蘭特原油跌破100美元/桶，支持美國公債利率持續走低至5%以下，自10月高點以來下滑
近50個基點。殖利率下滑輔以金融市場不確定因素降低支撐股市反彈，同步帶動美國信用債利差收斂，11月以
來美國信用債總報酬緩步走揚。

### Page 4

©  CTBC
兩黨對立將放大財政期限影響與紛擾，整體財政支出趨於中性
兩黨對立，財政期限的重要性上升
政策影響
2027年的財政動能預計將大致維持中性
4
• 財政期限的重要性上升，帶來政治紛擾：民主黨可利用具強制性的時間節點，作為政策讓步的槓桿。第一個要
注意的時間點是12/11政府支出授權到期，不過將期限延到2027年2至3月的機率較高(否則政府可能關門)。另
外推論1Q27將臨近債務上限，以目前的財政部存款和非常規措施的使用，最多可支應至2027年8月至10月。
• 財政政策整體趨於中性：分裂政府通常意味著較不具擴張性的財政政策，而眾議院掌握聯邦預算的撥款權，將
可能透過拒絕追加或直接限制戰爭預算的方式來迫使戰事降溫 。然民主黨同時也可能與共和黨協議，以撤銷去
年大而美法案的支出削減措施、恢復健康保險補貼等作為交換條件，讓白宮得以增加國防預算。綜合來看，短
線面臨預算協調的紛擾，美債殖利率波動仍可能偏高，但趨勢上政策制衡效果增強，有利減輕殖利率的壓力。
資料來源：左， 高盛，2026/11，右，高盛，2026/11。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11

### Page 5

©  CTBC
關稅結構可能改變，資料中心監管程度提高但不影響建置趨勢
民主黨將嘗試撤回部分關稅，但整體稅率變化有限
政策影響
對資料中心監管程度將提高，但非禁止建置
5
• 民主黨將嘗試撤回部分關稅 ，但整體稅率變化可能有限 ：民主黨領袖已表示將嘗試撤銷已實施的部分關稅，但
川普政府仍可利用301條款推動新的關稅，整體有效關稅稅率變化可能有限，但其中可能的重點是降低對加拿大
的關稅。
• 對資料中心監管程度將提高，不影響建置趨勢：傳統共和黨票倉的德州變天，而德州是最大的資料中心建設地
區，新任的民主黨州長可能要求企業自行承擔電力及電網擴建成本 、保障居民用水優先權，並在保護規範明確
之前影響資料中心的建設進度。不過本行認為，除了要求企業揭露資料來源與訓練資訊、建立安全治理架構，
以及落實其他風險管理措施等規定之外，尚無太多州級監管制度被認為具有高度限制性。而在聯邦層級，大規
模的新監管措施即使被國會提出，也很可能被川普否決，且AI競爭力仍是跨黨派國策，建設趨勢將持續推進。
資料來源：左， 高盛 ，2026/10，右，高盛，2026/10。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10

©  CTBC
AI 資料中心已從「產業政策」轉變為「民生電價議題」
超大型業者算力供給轉吃緊
產業影響
2030年電力缺口收斂
6
• 德州資料中心暫停令，AI算力布局的轉變與供需影響：德州8月初頒布的暫停令，同步凍結了新資料中心的興建
（僅限未動工新案）與電力輸送。在扣除被淘汰的虛報專案需求後，預估部分專案將外溢至其他電力更吃緊的州，
導致全美電力缺口淨擴大約 3–6 GW。若Hinojosa 就任後延續暫停令執行超過一年，其影響2028 年全美電力缺
口將進一步擴大，2030年供需缺口預估將逐步收斂。另一方面，暫停令讓2028年本就吃緊的算力市場更緊，維
持算力租金的高檔區間，對超大型業者而言建置成本增加，與手上既有的算力可以提高售價，兩者相抵，2028
年企業獲利的預估影響僅在1-3%水準。
• 提高監管是正在發生的實質逆風，喊停是低機率的極端政治口號：AI 技術攸關美國的核心基本國力。民主黨的
政策向來主張「調整與監管」，而非全面「廢止」。因此，未來更嚴格的許可審查與補助限制，勢必會提高雲

端大廠在德州擴張的時間成本與資本風險。然而，也可能推動針對資料中心的特定稅種，例如聯邦總收入稅，
或針對資料中心電力消耗的消費稅。民主黨也有可能在新會期提出比共和黨更強制性更嚴格監管的版本 。民主
黨掌控國會後，最可能成法的是「電費成本分攤」立法，因為川普已將此議題列為自己的政績。
資料來源：左，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11，右，blackrock，2026/4。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11/4

### Page 7

©  CTBC
健護最具兩黨協商的價值，醫療產業相對受惠
12/11為醫療補貼可以放進談判的時間點
產業影響
健護未來一年的本益比仍合理
7
• ACA補貼及醫療補助是最可能與川普達成交易的領域 ：民主黨贏得參眾兩院，不等於民主黨執政，兩黨仍須協
商。就健護產業而言，相對有利影響的領域在2025年底已到期的<平價醫療法案(ACA)>增強型保費補貼，受淨保
費上升，已有超過200萬人放棄投保 ，及受<大而美法案 >被削減的醫療補助 (Medicaid)在2025~2034年超過
9,000億美元。現ACA進行增強型補貼的恢復提案還在進行中，需總統簽署才能立法恢復，可以是談判交換條件
的一部分；Medicaid刪減是已成法、分階段生效，無法被推翻，只能延緩Medicaid的刪減，其中對工作的要求
限制於今年底生效，早於新國會就任，12/11臨時撥款法案談判是可以納入談判作為局部修正空間的時間點。
• 健護是民主黨國會最直接的受惠產業，健護未來一年本益比仍相對合理：預期若醫療補助刪減延後，醫院將受

惠，ACA補貼若部分恢復，將有利ACA型管理式醫療業者的投保人數止跌回升。以健護未來一年本益比來看，
目前股價仍相對合理。
資料來源：左，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11，右，LSEG，2026/11/4。 中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11/5

©  CTBC
資金輪動而未明顯離場，美股短線有壓然無礙趨勢
期中選舉前股市較為承壓，但無礙趨勢
市場觀點
資料來源：(左) 高盛，2026/11；(右) 摩根大通，2026/11 。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11
分裂國會在兩年任期內的表現優於單一政黨執政
8
1974年以來標普500指數期中選舉前後的走勢
• 期中選舉前股市較為承壓，但無礙趨勢：根據歷史經驗，期中選舉年通常是總統任期中回撤較深的一年，且選
前的股市波動會明顯放大，然在選後不確定性消退，市場表現往往有所改善，標普500指數在選後的3個月，歷
史中位數報酬率為+6%，而分裂政府(總統和國會分屬不同黨派)的歷史報酬率通常較佳。
• AI相關類股波動高，資金輪動而未明顯離場，美股短線有壓然無礙趨勢：真正驅動市場的因素仍是利率、地緣、
AI，而不是單純從歷史規律推演，近期市場擔憂雲端業者的現金流和債務問題，因此若資料中心的建置時程受
到影響，將進一步影響相關業者的營收和償債時程，提高AI相關類股的波動；另外眾議院是否對川普提出彈劾 ，

也將造成政治上的波動。但就整體趨勢來說，既有政策不會因此出現重大轉折，穩健成長的企業獲利提供重要
支撐，AI類股修正時期資金仍在其他類股間輪動而未明顯離場，美股短線有壓然無礙趨勢。
1950 年以來，不同政治格局下的標普500指數兩年價格報酬率
單一政黨掌握控制權

### Page 9

©  CTBC
短線待消化北亞出口疑慮，長線行情回歸基本面
情緒回穩後聚焦基本面與地緣動盪 短線消化資料中心建設監管趨嚴對北亞出口影響，
然長線美核心政策延續性依舊，估計影響有限
9
• 選後反彈高度取決景氣與政策推演：回顧過去美股期中選舉期間，新興市場選前壓抑，選後大多繳出正報酬佳
績，上漲比率超越五成。以參眾兩院控制權皆翻轉的1994年、2006年來看，1944年因美聯準會啟動快速升息，
加上墨西哥金融危機爆發，選後新興市場進入近一年空頭走勢。2006年即便也是分裂政府，全球宏觀環境受惠
貿易繁榮、商品超級周期推動經濟成長，新興市場選後12個月累積報酬亮眼。總體來說，選後不確定性下降，
新興市場可望迎反彈走勢，而即便美國政治版圖翻轉，選後新興市場走勢取決於景氣、政策推演與地緣干擾。
• 市場短線待消化資料中心建設進程對北亞出口影響：美期中選舉對新興市場影響較大面向，主要是關稅與貿易
政策、債務上限，以及對資料中心建設的態度。關稅主導權在白宮推估影響有限 ; 預算與債限談判或對利率造
成波動 ; 短線需消化選後對資料中心監管趨嚴影響北亞出口的失望情緒，然預計長線AI建置仍屬國家重心政策。

資料來源：左， Bloomberg ，2026/11，右， Bloomberg ，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11。

• 期中選舉共和黨大敗，美元走弱：週三市場確認共和黨於期中選舉失利後，美元兌主要貨幣全面回檔。本次期
中選舉由民主黨取得參、眾兩院多數。民主黨掌控參議院後，可阻止或延後川普的人事任命；贏得眾議院後，
則可對政府展開調查，甚至進一步推動彈劾程序。歷史經驗顯示，當民主黨於期中選舉後同時掌控參、眾兩院
時，美元指數平均下跌 4.6%，6次中有4次下跌。整體而言，選舉結果短期內可能因預算協商及債務上限僵局

升高政治風險。2027年美國將再度面臨債務上限問題，美伊地緣政治及貿易戰等變數亦將牽動實質利率走勢。
• 2027年債務上限恐成兩黨鬥爭焦點 ，不利美元：12月11日前，現任國會通過持續撥款決議的可能性偏高 ，但
美國聯邦政府收支預計將於2027年觸及債務上限。受債務上限約束的債務規模預估於2027年3月接近法定上限
500億美元以內 ，之後隨 4月稅收入帳而暫時下降；但若財政赤字增幅高於預期 ，X-Day恐提前到來 。展望
2027年，美國財政議題仍將面臨逆風。共和黨失去完全執政雖有助抑制財政擴張，但政治僵局恐加劇債務上限、
違約及信評遭調降風險，使美元波動加大。

## Document 564: 中国人民银行上调外汇存款准备金率点评：政策传递稳汇率信号

Source PDF: all report pdf/中国人民银行上调外汇存款准备金率点评：政策传递稳汇率信号.pdf
Report source: 万联证券
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

[Table_RightTitle] 
宏观研究|宏观快评报告

[Table_Title] 
政策传递稳汇率信号

[Table_ReportType] 
——中国人民银行上调外汇存款准备金率点评

[Table_ReportDate] 2026年11月03日

[Table_Summary] 投资要点： 
⚫ 事件：11月 3日，中国人民银行发布公告称，将于11月
15日，将外汇存款准备金率由现行的4%提高到6%。 
⚫ 人民币持续升值，政策意在平抑单边升值预期。在美元指
数保持强势的背景下，人民币今年以来持续升值趋势，美
元兑人民币中间价在 11月初达到6.65。近期升值斜率走
陡，如果短期升值过快，可能会对出口企业造成一定汇兑
损失。9月底我国外汇存款余额为 1.2万亿美元，此次上
调将收回约240亿美元左右的流动性，有助于间接平抑短
期人民币升值斜率，降低市场对人民币单边升值的预期，
并减轻对出口企业的影响。 
⚫ 政策重在释放稳汇率信号，不改中期汇率走势。从历史上
外汇存款准备金工具使用来看：1）2021年5月由5%上调
至7%，12月由7%上调至9%，主要是抑制人民币单边升值
的趋势。2）2005年7月汇改后，人民币持续升值，在
2006年9月由 3%上调至4%，2007年5月上调至 5%。3）
在2022年4月、9月和 2023年9月逐步下调外汇存款准
备金率，来缓和人民币持续贬值的趋势。但从中期来看，
人民币汇率走势仍将回归基本面定价。 
⚫ 出口强势带动企业结汇意愿走强，人民币仍处升值通道当
中。我国经济保持稳健，出口强势，贸易顺差持续扩张，
企业结售汇意愿走强，央行外汇占款同比随之走高，财政
出口退税增长。我国与美元指数的驱动因素分化，两者呈
现双双走强的态势。 
⚫ 美元保持强势，AI行情逐步成为美元定向新变量。美元
指数10月以来维持高位震荡，下半年美元指数和油价相
关性逐步走弱，而AI产业发展推升了美国经济增长预
期，股票估值上行，海外资本流入，带动了美股、美债和
美元指数上行。若AI相关资产价格大幅调整，或带来美
元指数下跌。中期选举对外汇市场的直接影响预期有限。 
⚫ 人民币或受政策影响短期贬值，长期升值趋势不变。历史
上调升外汇存款准备金率后，人民币在短期内出现贬值，
市场对国内的增长预期边际修正。央行平抑人民币升值斜
率，有助于减轻出口导向型企业的汇兑损益压力，保护出
口企业的盈利预期，并且稳汇率有助于稳定外资配置盘的
预期。后续人民币走势仍将取决于经济数据表现，在年末
